A pool may span more than one ResourceSlice, and exactly how many\n    54→// ResourceSlices comprise a pool is determined by the driver.\n    55→//\n    56→// At the moment, the only supported resources are devices with attributes and capacities.\n    57→// Each device in a given pool, regardless of how many ResourceSlices, must have a unique name.\n    58→// The ResourceSlice in which a device gets published may change over time. The unique identifier\n    59→// for a device is the tuple <driver name>, <pool name>, <device name>.\n    60→//\n    61→// Whenever a driver needs to update a pool, it increments the pool.Spec.Pool.Generation number\n    62→// and updates all ResourceSlices with that new number and new resource definitions. A consumer\n    63→// must only use ResourceSlices with the highest generation number and ignore all others.\n    64→//\n    65→// When allocating all resources in a pool matching certain criteria or when\n    66→// looking for the best solution among several different alternatives, a\n    67→// consumer should check the number of ResourceSlices in a pool (included in\n    68→// each ResourceSlice) to determine whether its view of a pool is complete and\n    69→// if not, should wait until the driver has completed updating the pool.\n    70→//\n    71→// For resources that are not local to a node, the node name is not set. Instead,\n    72→// the driver may use a node selector to specify where the devices are available.\n    73→//\n    74→// This is an alpha type and requires enabling the DynamicResourceAllocation\n    75→// feature gate.\n    76→type ResourceSlice struct {\n    77→\tmetav1.TypeMeta `json:\",inline\"`\n    78→\t// Standard object metadata\n    79→\t// +optional\n    80→\tmetav1.ObjectMeta `json:\"metadata,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=metadata\"`\n    81→\n    82→\t// Contains the information published by the driver.\n    83→\t//\n    84→\t// Changing the spec automatically increments the metadata.generation number.\n    85→\tSpec ResourceSliceSpec `json:\"spec\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,name=spec\"`\n    86→}\n    87→\n    88→const (\n    89→\t// ResourceSliceSelectorNodeName can be used in a [metav1.ListOptions]\n    90→\t// field selector to filter based on [ResourceSliceSpec.NodeName].\n    91→\tResourceSliceSelectorNodeName = \"spec.nodeName\"\n    92→\t// ResourceSliceSelectorDriver can be used in a [metav1.ListOptions]\n    93→\t// field selector to filter based on [ResourceSliceSpec.Driver].\n    94→\tResourceSliceSelectorDriver = \"spec.driver\"\n    95→)\n    96→\n    97→// ResourceSliceSpec contains the information published by the driver in one ResourceSlice.\n    98→type ResourceSliceSpec struct {\n    99→\t// Driver identifies the DRA driver providing the capacity information.\n   100→\t// A field selector can be used to list only ResourceSlice\n   101→\t// objects with a certain driver name.\n   102→\t//\n   103→\t// Must be a DNS subdomain and should end with a DNS domain owned by the\n   104→\t// vendor of the driver. not set, the requested amount is used as-is if it falls within the range Min to Max (if set).\n   512→//   - If the requested or rounded amount exceeds Max (if set), the request does not satisfy the With FirstAvailable it is also\n   794→// possible to provide a prioritized list of requests.\n   795→type DeviceRequest struct {\n   796→\t// Name can be used to reference this request in a pod.spec.containers[].resources.claims\n   797→\t// entry and in a constraint of the claim.\n   798→\t//\n   799→\t// References using the name in the DeviceRequest will uniquely\n   800→\t// identify a request when the Exactly field is set. It tries to\n   822→\t// satisfy them in the order in which they are listed here. So if\n   823→\t// there are two entries in the list, the scheduler will only check\n   824→\t// the second one if it determines that the first one can not be used.\n   825→\t//\n   826→\t// DRA does not yet implement scoring, so the scheduler will\n   827→\t// select the first set of devices that satisfies all the\n   828→\t// requests in the claim. And if the requirements can\n   829→\t// be satisfied on more than one node, other scheduling features\n   830→\t// will determine which node is chosen. This means that the set of\n   831→\t// devices allocated to a claim might not be the optimal set\n   832→\t// available to the cluster. Scoring will be implemented later.\n   833→\t//\n   834→\t// +optional\n   835→\t// +oneOf=deviceRequestType\n   836→\t// +listType=atomic\n   837→\t// +featureGate=DRAPrioritizedList\n   838→\t// +k8s:listType=atomic\n   839→\t// +k8s:unique=map\n   840→\t// +k8s:listMapKey=name\n   841→\t// +k8s:maxItems=8\n   842→\tFirstAvailable []DeviceSubRequest `json:\"firstAvailable,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,name=firstAvailable\"`\n   843→}\n   844→\n   845→// ExactDeviceRequest is a request for one or more identical devices.\n   846→type ExactDeviceRequest struct {\n   847→\t// DeviceClassName references a specific DeviceClass, which can define\n   848→\t// additional configuration and selectors to be inherited by this\n   849→\t// request.\n   850→\t//\n   851→\t// A DeviceClassName is required.\n   852→\t//\n   853→\t// Administrators may use this to restrict which devices may get\n   854→\t// requested by only installing classes with selectors for permitted\n   855→\t// devices. Supported values are:\n   874→\t//\n   875→\t// - ExactCount: This request is for a specific number of devices.\n   876→\t//   This is the default. The exact number is provided in the\n   877→\t//   count field.\n   878→\t//\n   879→\t// - All: This request is for all of the matching devices in a pool.\n   880→\t//   At least one device must exist on the node for the allocation to succeed.\n   881→\t//   Allocation will fail if some devices are already allocated,\n   882→\t//   unless adminAccess is requested.\n   883→\t//\n   884→\t// If AllocationMode is not specified, the default mode is ExactCount. If\n   885→\t// the mode is ExactCount and count is not specified, the default count is\n   886→\t// one. Any other requests must specify this field.\n   887→\t//\n   888→\t// More modes may get added in the future. Clients must refuse to handle\n   889→\t// requests with unknown modes.\n   890→\t//\n   891→\t// +optional\n   892→\t// +k8s:optional\n   893→\tAllocationMode DeviceAllocationMode `json:\"allocationMode,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,opt,name=allocationMode\"`\n   894→\n   895→\t// Count is used only when the count mode is \"ExactCount\". Must be greater than zero.\n   896→\t// If AllocationMode is ExactCount and this field is not specified, the default is one.\n   897→\t//\n   898→\t// +optional\n   899→\t// +oneOf=AllocationMode\n   900→\tCount int64 `json:\"count,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,4,opt,name=count\"`\n   901→\n   902→\t// AdminAccess indicates that this is a claim for administrative access\n   903→\t// to the device(s). Claims with AdminAccess are expected to be used for\n   904→\t// monitoring or other management services for a device.  They ignore\n   905→\t// all ordinary claims to the device with respect to access modes and\n   906→\t// any resource allocations.\n   907→\t//\n   908→\t// This is an alpha field and requires enabling the DRAAdminAccess\n   909→\t// feature gate. Admin access is disabled if this field is unset or\n   910→\t// set to false, otherwise it is enabled.\n   911→\t//\n   912→\t// +optional\n   913→\t// +featureGate=DRAAdminAccess\n   914→\tAdminAccess *bool `json:\"adminAccess,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,5,opt,name=adminAccess\"`\n   915→\n   916→\t// If specified, the request's tolerations.\n   917→\t//\n   918→\t// Tolerations for NoSchedule are required to allocate a\n   919→\t// device which has a taint with that effect. The same applies\n   920→\t// to NoExecute.\n   921→\t//\n   922→\t// In addition, should any of the allocated devices get tainted\n   923→\t// with NoExecute after allocation and that effect is not tolerated,\n   924→\t// then all pods consuming the ResourceClaim get deleted to evict\n   925→\t// them. The scheduler will not let new pods reserve the claim while\n   926→\t// it has these tainted devices. Once all pods are evicted, the\n   927→\t// claim will get deallocated.\n   928→\t//\n   929→\t// The maximum number of tolerations is 16.\n   930→\t//\n   931→\t// This is an alpha field and requires enabling the DRADeviceTaints\n   932→\t// feature gate.\n   933→\t//\n   934→\t// +optional\n   935→\t// +listType=atomic\n   936→\t// +featureGate=DRADeviceTaints\n   937→\tTolerations []DeviceToleration `json:\"tolerations,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,6,opt,name=tolerations\"`\n   938→\n   939→\t// Capacity define resource requirements against each capacity.\n   940→\t//\n   941→\t// If this field is unset and the device supports multiple allocations,\n   942→\t// the default value will be applied to each capacity according to requestPolicy.\n   943→\t// For the capacity that has no requestPolicy, default is the full capacity value.\n   944→\t//\n   945→\t// Applies to each device allocation.\n   946→\t// If Count > 1,\n   947→\t// the request fails if there aren't enough devices that meet the requirements.\n   948→\t// If AllocationMode is set to All,\n   949→\t// the request fails if there are devices that otherwise match the request,\n   950→\t// and have this capacity, with a value >= the requested amount, but which cannot be allocated to this request.\n   951→\t//\n   952→\t// +optional\n   953→\t// +featureGate=DRAConsumableCapacity\n   954→\tCapacity *CapacityRequirements `json:\"capacity,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,7,opt,name=capacity\"`\n   955→}\n   956→\n   957→// DeviceSubRequest describes a request for device provided in the\n   958→// claim.spec.devices.requests[].firstAvailable array. specific device request.\n  1072→type CapacityRequirements struct {\n  1073→\t// Requests represent individual device resource requests for distinct resources,\n  1074→\t// all of which must be provided by the device.\n  1075→\t//\n  1076→\t// This value is used as an additional filtering condition against the available capacity on the device.\n  1077→\t// This is semantically equivalent to a CEL selector with\n  1078→\t// `device.capacity[<domain>].<name>.compareTo(quantity(<request quantity>)) >= 0`.\n  1079→\t// For example, device.capacity['test-driver.cdi.k8s.io'].counters.compareTo(quantity('2')) >= 0.\n  1080→\t//\n  1081→\t// When a requestPolicy is defined, the requested amount is adjusted upward\n  1082→\t// to the nearest valid value based on the policy.\n  1083→\t// If the requested amount cannot be adjusted to a valid value—because it exceeds what the requestPolicy allows—\n  1084→\t// the device is considered ineligible for allocation.\n  1085→\t//\n  1086→\t// For any capacity that is not explicitly requested:\n  1087→\t// - If no requestPolicy is set, the default consumed capacity is equal to the full device capacity\n  1088→\t//   (i.e., the whole device is claimed).\n  1089→\t// - If a requestPolicy is set, the default consumed capacity is determined according to that policy.\n  1090→\t//\n  1091→\t// If the device allows multiple allocation,\n  1092→\t// the aggregated amount across all requests must not exceed the capacity value.\n  1093→\t// The consumed capacity, which may be adjusted based on the requestPolicy if defined,\n  1094→\t// is recorded in the resource claim’s status.devices[*].consumedCapacity field.\n  1095→\t//\n  1096→\t// +optional\n  1097→\tRequests    = DeviceAllocationMode(\"All\")\n  1114→)\n  1115→\n  1116→// DeviceSelector must have exactly one field set.\n  1117→type DeviceSelector struct {\n  1118→\t// CEL contains a CEL expression for 1124→\n  1125→// CELDeviceSelector contains a CEL expression for selecting a device.\n  1126→type CELDeviceSelector struct {\n  1127→\t// Expression is a CEL expression which evaluates a single device. It\n  1128→\t// must evaluate to true when the device under consideration satisfies\n  1129→\t// the desired criteria, and false when it does not. Any other result\n  1130→\t// is an error and causes allocation of devices to abort.\n  1131→\t//\n  1132→\t// The expression's input is an object named \"device\", which carries\n  1133→\t// the following properties:\n  1134→\t//  - driver (string): the name of the driver which defines this device.\n  1135→\t//  - attributes (map[string]object): the device's attributes, grouped by prefix\n  1136→\t//    (e.g. device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"] evaluates to an object with all\n  1137→\t//    of the attributes which were prefixed by \"dra.example.com\".\n  1138→\t//  - capacity (map[string]object): the device's capacities, grouped by prefix.\n  1139→\t//  - allowMultipleAllocations (bool): the allowMultipleAllocations property of the device\n  1140→\t//    (v1.34+ with the DRAConsumableCapacity feature enabled).\n  1141→\t//\n  1142→\t// Example: Consider a device with driver=\"dra.example.com\", which exposes\n  1143→\t// two attributes named \"model\" and \"ext.example.com/family\" and which\n  1144→\t// exposes one capacity named \"modules\". This input to this expression\n  1145→\t// would have the following fields:\n  1146→\t//\n  1147→\t//     device.driver\n  1148→\t//     device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"].model\n  1149→\t//     device.attributes[\"ext.example.com\"].family\n  1150→\t//     device.capacity[\"dra.example.com\"].modules\n  1151→\t//\n  1152→\t// The device.driver field can be used to check for a specific driver,\n  1153→\t// either as a high-level precondition (i.e. you only want to consider\n  1154→\t// devices from this driver) or as part of a multi-clause expression\n  1155→\t// that is meant to consider devices from different drivers.\n  1156→\t//\n  1157→\t// The value type of each attribute is defined by the device\n  1158→\t// definition, and users who write these expressions must consult the\n  1159→\t// documentation for their specific drivers. The value type of each\n  1160→\t// capacity is Quantity.\n  1161→\t//\n  1162→\t// If an unknown prefix is used as a lookup in either device.attributes\n  1163→\t// or device.capacity, an empty map will be returned. Any reference to\n  1164→\t// an unknown field will cause an evaluation error and allocation to\n  1165→\t// abort.\n  1166→\t//\n  1167→\t// A robust expression should check for the existence of attributes\n  1168→\t// before referencing them.\n  1169→\t//\n  1170→\t// For ease of use, the cel.bind() function is enabled, and can be used\n  1171→\t// to simplify expressions that access multiple attributes with the\n  1172→\t// same domain. For example:\n  1173→\t//\n  1174→\t//     cel.bind(dra, device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"], dra.someBool && dra.anotherBool)\n  1175→\t//\n  1176→\t// The length of the expression must be smaller or equal to 10 Ki. The\n  1177→\t// cost of evaluating it is also limited based on the estimated number\n  1178→\t// of logical steps.\n  1179→\t//\n  1180→\t// +required\n  1181→\tExpression string `json:\"expression\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,name=expression\"`\n  1182→}\n  1183→\n  1184→// CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost specifies the cost limit for a single CEL selector\n  1185→// evaluation.\n  1186→//\n  1187→// There is no overall budget for selecting a device, so the actual time\n  1188→// required for that is proportional to the number of CEL selectors and how\n  1189→// often they need to be evaluated, which can vary depending on several factors\n  1190→// (number of devices, cluster utilization, additional constraints).\n  1191→//\n  1192→// Validation against this limit and [CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength] happens\n  1193→// only when setting an expression for the first time or when changing it. If\n  1194→// the limits are changed in a future Kubernetes release, existing users are\n  1195→// guaranteed that existing expressions will continue to be valid.\n  1196→//\n  1197→// However, the kube-scheduler also applies this cost limit at runtime, so it\n  1198→// could happen that a valid expression fails at runtime after an up- or\n  1199→// downgrade. This can also happen without version skew when the cost estimate\n  1200→// underestimated the actual cost. That this might happen is the reason why\n  1201→// kube-scheduler enforces the runtime limit instead of relying on validation.\n  1202→//\n  1203→// According to\n  1204→// https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/blob/4aeaf1e99e82da8334c0d6dddd848a194cd44b4f/staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/apis/cel/config.go#L20-L22,\n  1205→// this gives roughly 0.1 second for each expression evaluation.\n  1206→// However, this depends on how fast the machine is.\n  1207→const CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost = 1000000\n  1208→\n  1209→// CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength is the maximum length of a CEL selector expression string.\n  1210→const CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength = 10 * 1024\n  1211→\n  1212→// DeviceConstraint must have exactly one field set besides Requests.\n  1213→type DeviceConstraint struct {\n  1214→\t// Requests is a list of the one or more requests in this claim which\n  1215→\t// must co-satisfy this constraint. If a request is fulfilled by\n  1216→\t// multiple devices, then all of the devices must satisfy the\n  1217→\t// constraint. 1263→\t// This acts as the inverse of MatchAttribute.\n  1264→\t//\n  1265→\t// This constraint is used to avoid allocating multiple requests to the same device\n  1266→\t// by ensuring It is the responsibility of\n  1326→\t// the driver developer to handle validation and versioning. Empty means match all taint keys.\n  1344→\t// If the key is empty, operator must be Exists; this combination means to match all values and all keys.\n  1345→\t// Must be a label name.\n  1346→\t//\n  1347→\t// +optional\n  1348→\t// +k8s:optional\n  1349→\t// +k8s:format=k8s-label-key\n  1350→\tKey string `json:\"key,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=key\"`\n  1351→\n  1352→\t// Operator represents a key's relationship to the value.\n  1353→\t// Valid operators are Exists and Equal. Defaults to Equal.\n  1354→\t// Exists is equivalent to wildcard for value, so that a ResourceClaim can\n  1355→\t// tolerate all taints of a particular category.\n  1356→\t//\n  1357→\t// +optional\n  1358→\t// +default=\"Equal\"\n  1359→\tOperator DeviceTolerationOperator `json:\"operator,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,opt,name=operator,casttype=DeviceTolerationOperator\"`\n  1360→\n  1361→\t// Value is the taint value the toleration matches to.\n  1362→\t// If the operator is Exists, the value must be empty, otherwise just a regular string.\n  1363→\t// Must be a label value.\n  1364→\t//\n  1365→\t// +optional\n  1366→\tValue string `json:\"value,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,opt,name=value\"`\n  1367→\n  1368→\t// Effect indicates the taint effect to match. Empty means match all taint effects.\n  1369→\t// When specified, allowed values are NoSchedule and NoExecute.\n  1370→\t//\n  1371→\t// +optional\n  1372→\tEffect DeviceTaintEffect `json:\"effect,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,4,opt,name=effect,casttype=DeviceTaintEffect\"`\n  1373→\n  1374→\t// TolerationSeconds represents the period of time the toleration (which must be\n  1375→\t// of effect NoExecute, otherwise this field is ignored) tolerates the taint. By default,\n  1376→\t// it is not set, which means tolerate the taint forever (do not evict). Zero and\n  1377→\t// negative values will be treated as 0 (evict immediately) by the system.\n  1378→\t// If larger than zero, the time when the pod needs to be evicted is calculated as <time when\n  1379→\t// taint was adedd> + <toleration seconds>.\n  1380→\t//\n  1381→\t// +optional\n  1382→\tTolerationSeconds *int64 `json:\"tolerationSeconds,omitempty\" protobuf:\"varint,5,opt,name=tolerationSeconds\"`\n  1383→}\n  1384→\n  1385→// A toleration operator is the set of operators that can be used in a toleration.\n  1386→//\n  1387→// +enum\n  1388→// +k8s:enum\n  1389→type DeviceTolerationOperator string\n  1390→\n  1391→const (\n  1392→\tDeviceTolerationOpExists DeviceTolerationOperator = \"Exists\"\n  1393→\tDeviceTolerationOpEqual  DeviceTolerationOperator = \"Equal\"\n  1394→)\n  1395→\n  1396→// ResourceClaimStatus tracks whether the resource has been allocated and what\n  1397→// the result of that was.\n  1398→type ResourceClaimStatus struct {\n  1399→\t// Allocation is set once the claim has been allocated successfully.\n  1400→\t//\n  1401→\t// +optional\n  1402→\t// +k8s:optional\n  1403→\t// +k8s:update=NoModify\n  1404→\tAllocation *AllocationResult `json:\"allocation,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=allocation\"`\n  1405→\n  1406→\t// ReservedFor indicates which entities are currently allowed to use\n  1407→\t// the claim. A Pod which references a ResourceClaim which is not\n  1408→\t// reserved for that Pod will not be started. A claim that is in\n  1409→\t// use or might be in use because it has been reserved must not get\n  1410→\t// deallocated.\n  1411→\t//\n  1412→\t// In a cluster with multiple scheduler instances, two pods might get\n  1413→\t// scheduled concurrently by different schedulers. When they reference\n  1414→\t// the same ResourceClaim which already has reached its maximum number\n  1415→\t// of consumers, only one pod can be scheduled.\n  1416→\t//\n  1417→\t// Both schedulers try to add their pod to the claim.status.reservedFor\n  1418→\t// field, but only the update that reaches the API server first gets\n  1419→\t// stored. The other one fails with an error and the scheduler\n  1420→\t// which issued it knows that it must put the pod back into the queue,\n  1421→\t// waiting for the ResourceClaim to become usable again.\n  1422→\t//\n  1423→\t// There can be at most 256 such reservations. This may get increased in\n  1424→\t// the future, but not reduced.\n  1425→\t//\n  1426→\t// +optional\n  1427→\t// +listType=map\n  1428→\t// +listMapKey=uid\n  1429→\t// +patchStrategy=merge\n  1430→\t// +patchMergeKey=uid\n  1431→\t// +k8s:optional\n  1432→\t// +k8s:listType=map\n  1433→\t// +k8s:listMapKey=uid\n  1434→\t// +k8s:maxItems=256\n  1435→\tReservedFor []ResourceClaimConsumerReference `json:\"reservedFor,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,opt,name=reservedFor\" patchStrategy:\"merge\" patchMergeKey:\"uid\"`\n  1436→\n  1437→\t// DeallocationRequested is tombstoned since Kubernetes 1.32 where\n  1438→\t// it got removed. A pool may span more than one ResourceSlice, and exactly how many\n    54→// ResourceSlices comprise a pool is determined by the driver.\n    55→//\n    56→// At the moment, the only supported resources are devices with attributes and capacities.\n    57→// Each device in a given pool, regardless of how many ResourceSlices, must have a unique name.\n    58→// The ResourceSlice in which a device gets published may change over time. The unique identifier\n    59→// for a device is the tuple <driver name>, <pool name>, <device name>.\n    60→//\n    61→// Whenever a driver needs to update a pool, it increments the pool.Spec.Pool.Generation number\n    62→// and updates all ResourceSlices with that new number and new resource definitions. A consumer\n    63→// must only use ResourceSlices with the highest generation number and ignore all others.\n    64→//\n    65→// When allocating all resources in a pool matching certain criteria or when\n    66→// looking for the best solution among several different alternatives, a\n    67→// consumer should check the number of ResourceSlices in a pool (included in\n    68→// each ResourceSlice) to determine whether its view of a pool is complete and\n    69→// if not, should wait until the driver has completed updating the pool.\n    70→//\n    71→// For resources that are not local to a node, the node name is not set. Instead,\n    72→// the driver may use a node selector to specify where the devices are available.\n    73→//\n    74→// This is an alpha type and requires enabling the DynamicResourceAllocation\n    75→// feature gate.\n    76→type ResourceSlice struct {\n    77→\tmetav1.TypeMeta `json:\",inline\"`\n    78→\t// Standard object metadata\n    79→\t// +optional\n    80→\tmetav1.ObjectMeta `json:\"metadata,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=metadata\"`\n    81→\n    82→\t// Contains the information published by the driver.\n    83→\t//\n    84→\t// Changing the spec automatically increments the metadata.generation number.\n    85→\tSpec ResourceSliceSpec `json:\"spec\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,name=spec\"`\n    86→}\n    87→\n    88→const (\n    89→\t// ResourceSliceSelectorNodeName can be used in a [metav1.ListOptions]\n    90→\t// field selector to filter based on [ResourceSliceSpec.NodeName].\n    91→\tResourceSliceSelectorNodeName = \"spec.nodeName\"\n    92→\t// ResourceSliceSelectorDriver can be used in a [metav1.ListOptions]\n    93→\t// field selector to filter based on [ResourceSliceSpec.Driver].\n    94→\tResourceSliceSelectorDriver = \"spec.driver\"\n    95→)\n    96→\n    97→// ResourceSliceSpec contains the information published by the driver in one ResourceSlice.\n    98→type ResourceSliceSpec struct {\n    99→\t// Driver identifies the DRA driver providing the capacity information.\n   100→\t// A field selector can be used to list only ResourceSlice\n   101→\t// objects with a certain driver name.\n   102→\t//\n   103→\t// Must be a DNS subdomain and should end with a DNS domain owned by the\n   104→\t// vendor of the driver. 475→\t// ValidValues defines a set of acceptable quantity values in consuming requests.\n   476→\t//\n   477→\t// Must not contain more than 10 entries.\n   478→\t// Must be sorted in ascending   510→// CapacityRequestPolicyRange defines a valid range for consumable capacity values.\n   511→//\n   512→//   - If the requested amount is less than Min, it is rounded up to the Min value.\n   513→//   - If Step is set and the requested amount is between Min and Max but not aligned with Step,\n   514→//     it will be rounded up to the next value equal to Min + (n * Step).\n   515→//   - If Step is not set, the requested amount is used as-is if it falls within the range Min to Max (if set).\n   516→//   - If the requested or rounded amount exceeds Max (if set), the request does not satisfy the policy,\n   517→//     and the device cannot be allocated.\n   518→type CapacityRequestPolicyRange struct {\n   519→\t// Min specifies the minimum capacity allowed for a consumption request.\n   520→\t//\n   521→\t// Min must be greater than or equal to zero,\n   522→\t// and less than or equal to the capacity value.\n   523→\t// requestPolicy.default must be more than or equal amounts within the range.\n   537→\t//\n   538→\t// Max (if set) and requestPolicy.default must be a multiple of Step.\n   539→\t// Min + Step must be less than or equal to the capacity value.\n   540→\t//\n   541→\t// +optional\n   542→\tStep *resource.Quantity `json:\"step,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,opt,name=step\"`\n   543→}\n   544→\n   545→// Limit for the sum of the number of entries This is\n   774→\t// fine because scoring allows users to define a preference, without\n   775→\t// making it a hard requirement.\n   776→\t//\n   777→\t// Score *SomeScoringStruct\n   778→}\n   779→\n   780→const (\n   781→\tDeviceRequestsMaxSize    = AllocationResultsMaxSize\n   782→\tDeviceConstraintsMaxSize = 32\n   783→\tDeviceConfigMaxSize      = 32\n   784→)\n   785→\n   786→// DRAAdminNamespaceLabelKey is a label key used to grant administrative access\n   787→// to certain resource.k8s.io API types within a namespace. When this label is\n   788→// set on a namespace with the value \"true\" (case-sensitive), it allows the use\n   789→// of adminAccess: true in any namespaced resource.k8s.io API types. Currently,\n   790→// this permission applies to ResourceClaim and ResourceClaimTemplate objects.\n   791→const (\n   792→\tDRAAdminNamespaceLabelKey = \"resource.kubernetes.io/admin-access\"\n   793→)\n   794→\n   795→// DeviceRequest is a request for devices required for a claim.\n   796→// This is typically a request for a single resource like a device, but can\n   797→// also ask for several identical devices.\n   798→type DeviceRequest struct {\n   799→\t// Name can be used to reference this request in a pod.spec.containers[].resources.claims\n   800→\t// entry and in a constraint of the claim.\n   801→\t//\n   802→\t// Must be a DNS label and unique among all DeviceRequests in a\n   803→\t// ResourceClaim.\n   804→\t//\n   805→\t// +required\n   806→\tName string `json:\"name\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,name=name\"`\n   807→\n   808→\t// DeviceClassName references a specific DeviceClass, which can define\n   809→\t// additional configuration and selectors to be inherited by this\n   810→\t// request.\n   811→\t//\n   812→\t// A class is required if no subrequests are specified in the\n   813→\t// firstAvailable list and no class can be set if subrequests\n   814→\t// are specified in the firstAvailable list.\n   815→\t// Which classes are available depends on the cluster.\n   816→\t//\n   817→\t// Administrators may use this to restrict which devices may get\n   818→\t// requested by only installing classes with selectors for permitted\n   819→\t// devices. It tries to\n   895→\t// satisfy them in the order in which they are listed here. So if\n   896→\t// there are two entries in the list, the scheduler will only check\n   897→\t// the second one if it determines that the first one cannot be used.\n   898→\t//\n   899→\t// This field may only be set in the entries of DeviceClaim.Requests.\n   900→\t//\n   901→\t// DRA does not yet implement scoring, so the scheduler will\n   902→\t// select the first set of devices that satisfies all the\n   903→\t// requests in the claim. specific device request.\n  1079→type CapacityRequirements struct {\n  1080→\t// Requests represent individual device resource requests for distinct resources,\n  1081→\t// all of which must be provided by the device.\n  1082→\t//\n  1083→\t// This value is used as an additional filtering condition against the available capacity on the device.\n  1084→\t// This is semantically equivalent to a CEL selector with\n  1085→\t// `device.capacity[<domain>].<name>.compareTo(quantity(<request quantity>)) >= 0`.\n  1086→\t// For example, device.capacity['test-driver.cdi.k8s.io'].counters.compareTo(quantity('2')) >= 0.\n  1087→\t//\n  1088→\t// When a requestPolicy is defined, the requested amount is adjusted upward\n  1089→\t// to the nearest valid value based on the policy.\n  1090→\t// If the requested amount cannot be adjusted to a valid value—because it exceeds what the requestPolicy allows—\n  1091→\t// the device is considered ineligible for allocation.\n  1092→\t//\n  1093→\t// For any capacity that is not explicitly requested:\n  1094→\t// - If no requestPolicy is set, the default consumed capacity is equal to the full device capacity\n  1095→\t//   (i.e., the whole device is claimed).\n  1096→\t// - If a requestPolicy is set, the default consumed capacity is determined according to that policy.\n  1097→\t//\n  1098→\t// If the device allows multiple allocation,\n  1099→\t// the aggregated amount across all requests must not exceed the capacity value.\n  1100→\t// The consumed capacity, which may be adjusted based on the requestPolicy if defined,\n  1101→\t// is recorded in the resource claim’s status.devices[*].consumedCapacity field.\n  1102→\t//\n  1103→\t// +optional\n  1104→\tRequests map[QualifiedName]resource.Quantity `json:\"requests,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,rep,name=requests,castkey=QualifiedName\"`\n  1105→}\n  1106→\n  1107→const (\n  1108→\tDeviceSelectorsMaxSize             = 32\n  1109→\tFirstAvailableDeviceRequestMaxSize = 8\n  1110→\tDeviceTolerationsMaxLength         = 16\n  1111→)\n  1112→\n  1113→// +enum\n  1114→// +k8s:enum\n  1115→type DeviceAllocationMode string\n  1116→\n  1117→// Valid [DeviceRequest.CountMode] values.\n  1118→const (\n  1119→\tDeviceAllocationModeExactCount = DeviceAllocationMode(\"ExactCount\")\n  1120→\tDeviceAllocationModeAll        = DeviceAllocationMode(\"All\")\n  1121→)\n  1122→\n  1123→// DeviceSelector must have exactly one field set.\n  1124→type DeviceSelector struct {\n  1125→\t// CEL contains a CEL expression for selecting a device.\n  1126→\t//\n  1127→\t// +optional\n  1128→\t// +oneOf=SelectorType\n  1129→\tCEL *CELDeviceSelector `json:\"cel,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=cel\"`\n  1130→}\n  1131→\n  1132→// CELDeviceSelector contains a CEL expression for selecting a device.\n  1133→type CELDeviceSelector struct {\n  1134→\t// Expression is a CEL expression which evaluates a single device. It\n  1135→\t// must evaluate to true when the device under consideration satisfies\n  1136→\t// the desired criteria, and false when it does not. Any other result\n  1137→\t// is an error and causes allocation of devices to abort.\n  1138→\t//\n  1139→\t// The expression's input is an object named \"device\", which carries\n  1140→\t// the following properties:\n  1141→\t//  - driver (string): the name of the driver which defines this device.\n  1142→\t//  - attributes (map[string]object): the device's attributes, grouped by prefix\n  1143→\t//    (e.g. device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"] evaluates to an object with all\n  1144→\t//    of the attributes which were prefixed by \"dra.example.com\".\n  1145→\t//  - capacity (map[string]object): the device's capacities, grouped by prefix.\n  1146→\t//  - allowMultipleAllocations (bool): the allowMultipleAllocations property of the device\n  1147→\t//    (v1.34+ with the DRAConsumableCapacity feature enabled).\n  1148→\t//\n  1149→\t// Example: Consider a device with driver=\"dra.example.com\", which exposes\n  1150→\t// two attributes named \"model\" and \"ext.example.com/family\" and which\n  1151→\t// exposes one capacity named \"modules\". This input to this expression\n  1152→\t// would have the following fields:\n  1153→\t//\n  1154→\t//     device.driver\n  1155→\t//     device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"].model\n  1156→\t//     device.attributes[\"ext.example.com\"].family\n  1157→\t//     device.capacity[\"dra.example.com\"].modules\n  1158→\t//\n  1159→\t// The device.driver field can be used to check for a specific driver,\n  1160→\t// either as a high-level precondition (i.e. you only want to consider\n  1161→\t// devices from this driver) or as part of a multi-clause expression\n  1162→\t// that is meant to consider devices from different drivers.\n  1163→\t//\n  1164→\t// The value type of each attribute is defined by the device\n  1165→\t// definition, and users who write these expressions must consult the\n  1166→\t// documentation for their specific drivers. The value type of each\n  1167→\t// capacity is Quantity.\n  1168→\t//\n  1169→\t// If an unknown prefix is used as a lookup in either device.attributes\n  1170→\t// or device.capacity, an empty map will be returned. Any reference to\n  1171→\t// an unknown field will cause an evaluation error and allocation to\n  1172→\t// abort.\n  1173→\t//\n  1174→\t// A robust expression should check for the existence of attributes\n  1175→\t// before referencing them.\n  1176→\t//\n  1177→\t// For ease of use, the cel.bind() function is enabled, and can be used\n  1178→\t// to simplify expressions that access multiple attributes with the\n  1179→\t// same domain. For example:\n  1180→\t//\n  1181→\t//     cel.bind(dra, device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"], dra.someBool && dra.anotherBool)\n  1182→\t//\n  1183→\t// The length of the expression must be smaller or equal to 10 Ki. The\n  1184→\t// cost of evaluating it is also limited based on the estimated number\n  1185→\t// of logical steps.\n  1186→\t//\n  1187→\t// +required\n  1188→\tExpression string `json:\"expression\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,name=expression\"`\n  1189→}\n  1190→\n  1191→// CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost specifies the cost limit for a single CEL selector\n  1192→// evaluation.\n  1193→//\n  1194→// There is no overall budget for selecting a device, so the actual time\n  1195→// required for that is proportional to the number of CEL selectors and how\n  1196→// often they need to be evaluated, which can vary depending on several factors\n  1197→// (number of devices, cluster utilization, additional constraints).\n  1198→//\n  1199→// Validation against this limit and [CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength] happens\n  1200→// only when setting an expression for the first time or when changing it. If\n  1201→// the limits are changed in a future Kubernetes release, existing users are\n  1202→// guaranteed that existing expressions will continue to be valid.\n  1203→//\n  1204→// However, the kube-scheduler also applies this cost limit at runtime, so it\n  1205→// could happen that a valid expression fails at runtime after an up- or\n  1206→// downgrade. This can also happen without version skew when the cost estimate\n  1207→// underestimated the actual cost. That this might happen is the reason why\n  1208→// kube-scheduler enforces the runtime limit instead of relying on validation.\n  1209→//\n  1210→// According to\n  1211→// https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/blob/4aeaf1e99e82da8334c0d6dddd848a194cd44b4f/staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/apis/cel/config.go#L20-L22,\n  1212→// this gives roughly 0.1 second for each expression evaluation.\n  1213→// However, this depends on how fast the machine is.\n  1214→const CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost = 1000000\n  1215→\n  1216→// CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength is the maximum length of a CEL selector expression string.\n  1217→const CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength = 10 * 1024\n  1218→\n  1219→// DeviceConstraint must have exactly one field set besides Requests.\n  1220→type DeviceConstraint struct {\n  1221→\t// Requests is a list of the one or more requests in this claim which\n  1222→\t// must co-satisfy this constraint. If a request is fulfilled by\n  1223→\t// multiple devices, then all of the devices must satisfy the\n  1224→\t// constraint. If this is not specified, this constraint applies to all\n  1225→\t// requests in this claim.\n  1226→\t//\n  1227→\t// References to subrequests must include the name of the main request\n  1228→\t// and may include the subrequest using the format <main request>[/<subrequest>]. If just\n  1229→\t// the main request is given, the constraint applies to all subrequests.\n  1230→\t//\n  1231→\t// +optional\n  1232→\t// +listType=atomic\n  1233→\t// +k8s:listType=atomic\n  1234→\t// +k8s:unique=set\n  1235→\t// +k8s:maxItems=32\n  1236→\tRequests []string `json:\"requests,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=requests\"`\n  1237→\n  1238→\t// MatchAttribute requires that all devices in question have this\n  1239→\t// attribute and that its type and value are the same across those\n  1240→\t// devices.\n  1241→\t//\n  1242→\t// For example, if you specified \"dra.example.com/numa\" (a hypothetical example!),\n  1243→\t// then only devices in the same NUMA node will be chosen. A device which\n  1244→\t// does not have that attribute will not be chosen. All devices should\n  1245→\t// use a value of the same type for this attribute because that is part of\n  1246→\t// its specification, but if one device doesn't, then it also will not be\n  1247→\t// chosen.\n  1248→\t//\n  1249→\t// Must include the domain qualifier.\n  1250→\t//\n  1251→\t// +optional\n  1252→\t// +oneOf=ConstraintType\n  1253→\t// +k8s:optional\n  1254→\t// +k8s:format=k8s-resource-fully-qualified-name\n  1255→\tMatchAttribute *FullyQualifiedName `json:\"matchAttribute,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,opt,name=matchAttribute\"`\n  1256→\n  1257→\t// Potential future extension, not part of the current design:\n  1258→\t// A CEL expression which compares different devices and returns\n  1259→\t// true if they match.\n  1260→\t//\n  1261→\t// Because it would be part of a one-of, old schedulers will not\n  1262→\t// accidentally ignore this additional, for them unknown match\n  1263→\t// criteria.\n  1264→\t//\n  1265→\t// MatchExpression string\n  1266→\n  1267→\t// DistinctAttribute requires that all devices in question have this\n  1268→\t// attribute and that its type and value are unique across those devices.\n  1269→\t//\n  1270→\t// This acts as the inverse of MatchAttribute.\n  1271→\t//\n  1272→\t// This constraint is used to avoid allocating multiple requests to the same device\n  1273→\t// by ensuring attribute-level differentiation.\n  1274→\t//\n  1275→\t// This is useful for scenarios where resource requests must be fulfilled by separate physical devices.\n  1276→\t// For example, a container requests two network interfaces that must be allocated from two different physical NICs.\n  1277→\t//\n  1278→\t// +optional\n  1279→\t// +oneOf=ConstraintType\n  1280→\t// +featureGate=DRAConsumableCapacity\n  1281→\tDistinctAttribute *FullyQualifiedName `json:\"distinctAttribute,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,opt,name=distinctAttribute\"`\n  1282→}\n  1283→\n  1284→// Empty means match all taint keys.\n  1351→\t// If the key is empty, operator must be Exists; this combination means to match all values and all keys.\n  1352→\t// Must be a label name.\n  1353→\t//\n  1354→\t// +optional\n  1355→\t// +k8s:optional\n  1356→\t// +k8s:format=k8s-label-key\n  1357→\tKey string `json:\"key,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=key\"`\n  1358→\n  1359→\t// Operator represents a key's relationship to the value.\n  1360→\t// Valid operators are Exists and Equal. Defaults to Equal.\n  1361→\t// Exists is equivalent to wildcard for value, so that a ResourceClaim can\n  1362→\t// tolerate all taints of a particular category.\n  1363→\t//\n  1364→\t// +optional\n  1365→\t// +default=\"Equal\"\n  1366→\tOperator DeviceTolerationOperator `json:\"operator,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,opt,name=operator,casttype=DeviceTolerationOperator\"`\n  1367→\n  1368→\t// Value is the taint value the toleration matches to.\n  1369→\t// If the operator is Exists, the value must be empty, otherwise just a regular string.\n  1370→\t// Must be a label value.\n  1371→\t//\n  1372→\t// +optional\n  1373→\tValue string `json:\"value,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,opt,name=value\"`\n  1374→\n  1375→\t// Effect indicates the taint effect to match. By default,\n  1383→\t// it is not set, which means tolerate the taint forever (do not evict). A Pod which references a ResourceClaim which is not\n  1415→\t// reserved for that Pod will not be started. A claim that is in\n  1416→\t// use or might be in use because it has been reserved must not get\n  1417→\t// deallocated.\n  1418→\t//\n  1419→\t// In a cluster with multiple scheduler instances, two pods might get\n  1420→\t// scheduled concurrently by different schedulers. When they reference\n  1421→\t// the same ResourceClaim which already has reached its maximum number\n  1422→\t// of consumers, only one pod can be scheduled.\n  1423→\t//\n  1424→\t// Both schedulers try to add their pod to the claim.status.reservedFor\n  1425→\t// field, but only the update that reaches the API server first gets\n  1426→\t// stored. A pool may span more than one ResourceSlice, and exactly how many\n    54→// ResourceSlices comprise a pool is determined by the driver.\n    55→//\n    56→// At the moment, the only supported resources are devices with attributes and capacities.\n    57→// Each device in a given pool, regardless of how many ResourceSlices, must have a unique name.\n    58→// The ResourceSlice in which a device gets published may change over time. The unique identifier\n    59→// for a device is the tuple <driver name>, <pool name>, <device name>.\n    60→//\n    61→// Whenever a driver needs to update a pool, it increments the pool.Spec.Pool.Generation number\n    62→// and updates all ResourceSlices with that new number and new resource definitions. A consumer\n    63→// must only use ResourceSlices with the highest generation number and ignore all others.\n    64→//\n    65→// When allocating all resources in a pool matching certain criteria or when\n    66→// looking for the best solution among several different alternatives, a\n    67→// consumer should check the number of ResourceSlices in a pool (included in\n    68→// each ResourceSlice) to determine whether its view of a pool is complete and\n    69→// if not, should wait until the driver has completed updating the pool.\n    70→//\n    71→// For resources that are not local to a node, the node name is not set. Instead,\n    72→// the driver may use a node selector to specify where the devices are available.\n    73→//\n    74→// This is an alpha type and requires enabling the DynamicResourceAllocation\n    75→// feature gate.\n    76→type ResourceSlice struct {\n    77→\tmetav1.TypeMeta `json:\",inline\"`\n    78→\t// Standard object metadata\n    79→\t// +optional\n    80→\tmetav1.ObjectMeta `json:\"metadata,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=metadata\"`\n    81→\n    82→\t// Contains the information published by the driver.\n    83→\t//\n    84→\t// Changing the spec automatically increments the metadata.generation number.\n    85→\tSpec ResourceSliceSpec `json:\"spec\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,name=spec\"`\n    86→}\n    87→\n    88→const (\n    89→\t// ResourceSliceSelectorNodeName can be used in a [metav1.ListOptions]\n    90→\t// field selector to filter based on [ResourceSliceSpec.NodeName].\n    91→\tResourceSliceSelectorNodeName = \"spec.nodeName\"\n    92→\t// ResourceSliceSelectorDriver can be used in a [metav1.ListOptions]\n    93→\t// field selector to filter based on [ResourceSliceSpec.Driver].\n    94→\tResourceSliceSelectorDriver = \"spec.driver\"\n    95→)\n    96→\n    97→// ResourceSliceSpec contains the information published by the driver in one ResourceSlice.\n    98→type ResourceSliceSpec struct {\n    99→\t// Driver identifies the DRA driver providing the capacity information.\n   100→\t// A field selector can be used to list only ResourceSlice\n   101→\t// objects with a certain driver name.\n   102→\t//\n   103→\t// Must be a DNS subdomain and should end with a DNS domain owned by the\n   104→\t// vendor of the driver. If it is set to true, every device defined the ResourceSlice\n   162→\t// must specify this individually.\n   163→\t//\n   164→\t// Exactly one of NodeName, NodeSelector, AllNodes, and PerDeviceNodeSelection must be set.\n   165→\t//\n   166→\t// +optional\n   167→\t// +oneOf=NodeSelection\n   168→\t// +featureGate=DRAPartitionableDevices\n   169→\tPerDeviceNodeSelection *bool `json:\"perDeviceNodeSelection,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,7,name=perDeviceNodeSelection\"`\n   170→\n   171→\t// SharedCounters defines a list of counter sets, each of which\n   172→\t// has a name and a list of counters available.\n   173→\t//\n   174→\t// The names of the SharedCounters must be unique in the ResourceSlice.\n   175→\t//\n   176→\t// The maximum number of counters in all sets is 32.\n   177→\t//\n   178→\t// +optional\n   179→\t// +listType=atomic\n   180→\t// +featureGate=DRAPartitionableDevices\n   181→\tSharedCounters []CounterSet `json:\"sharedCounters,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,8,name=sharedCounters\"`\n   182→}\n   183→\n   184→// CounterSet defines a named set of counters\n   185→// that are available to be used by devices defined in the\n   186→// ResourceSlice.\n   187→//\n   188→// The counters are not allocatable by themselves, but\n   189→// can be referenced by devices. For node-local devices, this\n   217→\t// is often the node name, but this is not required.\n   218→\t//\n   219→\t// It must not be longer than 253 characters and must consist of one or more DNS sub-domains\n   220→\t// separated by slashes. This field is immutable.\n   221→\t//\n   222→\t// +required\n   223→\tName string `json:\"name\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,name=name\"`\n   224→\n   225→\t// Generation tracks the change in a pool over time. Whenever a driver\n   226→\t// changes something about one or more of the resources in a pool, it\n   227→\t// must change the generation in all ResourceSlices which are part of\n   228→\t// that pool. Consumers of ResourceSlices should only consider\n   229→\t// resources from the pool with the highest generation number. The\n   230→\t// generation may be reset by drivers, which should be fine for\n   231→\t// consumers, assuming that all ResourceSlices in a pool are updated to\n   232→\t// match or deleted.\n   233→\t//\n   234→\t// Combined with ResourceSliceCount, this mechanism enables consumers to\n   235→\t// detect pools which are comprised of multiple ResourceSlices and are\n   236→\t// in an incomplete state.\n   237→\t//\n   238→\t// +required\n   239→\tGeneration int64 `json:\"generation\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,name=generation\"`\n   240→\n   241→\t// ResourceSliceCount is the total number of ResourceSlices in the pool at this\n   242→\t// generation number. 405→// DeviceCounterConsumption defines a set of counters that\n   406→// a device will consume from a CounterSet.\n   407→type DeviceCounterConsumption struct {\n   408→\t// CounterSet is the name of  413→\n   414→\t// Counters defines the counters that will be consumed by the device.\n   415→\t//\n   416→\t// The maximum number counters in a device is 32.\n   417→\t// In addition, the maximum +required\n   436→\tValue resource.Quantity `json:\"value\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,rep,name=value\"`\n   437→\n   438→\t// RequestPolicy defines how this DeviceCapacity must be consumed\n   439→\t// when the device is allowed to be shared by multiple allocations.\n   440→\t//\n   441→\t// The Device must have allowMultipleAllocations set to true in order to set a requestPolicy.\n   442→\t//\n   443→\t// If unset, capacity requests are unconstrained:\n   444→\t// requests can consume any amount of capacity, as long as the total consumed\n   445→\t// across all allocations does not exceed the `json:\"value\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,rep,name=value\"`\n   459→}\n   460→\n   461→// CapacityRequestPolicy defines how requests consume device capacity.\n   462→//\n   463→// Must not set more than one ValidRequestValues.\n   464→type CapacityRequestPolicy struct {\n   465→\t// Default specifies how much of this capacity is consumed by a request\n   466→\t// that does not contain an entry for it in DeviceRequest's Capacity.\n   467→\t//\n   468→\t// +optional\n   469→\tDefault *resource.Quantity `json:\"default\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=default\"`\n   470→\n   471→\t// ValidValues defines a set of acceptable quantity values in consuming requests.\n   472→\t//\n   473→\t// Must not contain more than 10 entries.\n   474→\t// Must be sorted in ascending order.\n   475→\t//\n   476→\t// If this field is set,\n   477→\t// Default must be defined and it must be included in ValidValues list.\n   478→\t//\n   479→\t// If the requested amount does not match any valid value but smaller than some valid values,\n   480→\t// the scheduler calculates the smallest valid value that is greater than or equal to the request.\n   481→\t// That is: min(ceil(requestedValue) ∈ validValues), where requestedValue ≤ max(validValues).\n   482→\t//\n   483→\t// If the requested amount exceeds all valid values, the request violates the policy,\n   484→\t// and this device cannot be allocated.\n   485→\t//\n   486→\t// +optional\n   487→\t// +listType=atomic\n   488→\t// +oneOf=ValidRequestValues\n   489→\tValidValues []resource.Quantity `json:\"validValues,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,opt,name=validValues\"`\n   490→\n   491→\t// ValidRange defines an acceptable quantity value range in consuming requests.\n   492→\t//\n   493→\t// If this field is set,\n   494→\t// Default must be defined and it must fall within the defined ValidRange.\n   495→\t//\n   496→\t// If the requested amount does not fall within the defined range, the request violates the policy,\n   497→\t// and this device cannot be allocated.\n   498→\t//\n   499→\t// If the request doesn't contain this capacity entry, Default value is used.\n   500→\t//\n   501→\t// +optional\n   502→\t// +oneOf=ValidRequestValues\n   503→\tValidRange *CapacityRequestPolicyRange `json:\"validRange,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,4,opt,name=validRange\"`\n   504→}\n   505→\n   506→// CapacityRequestPolicyRange defines a valid range for consumable capacity values.\n   507→//\n   508→//   - If the requested amount is less than Min, it is rounded up to the Min value.\n   509→//   - If Step is set and the requested amount is between Min and Max but not aligned with Step,\n   510→//     it will be rounded up to the next value equal to Min + (n * Step).\n   511→//   - If Step is not set, the requested amount is used as-is if it falls within the range Min to Max (if set).\n   512→//   - If the requested or rounded amount exceeds Max (if set), the request does not satisfy the policy,\n   513→//     and the device cannot be allocated.\n   514→type CapacityRequestPolicyRange struct {\n   515→\t// Min specifies the minimum capacity allowed for a consumption request.\n   516→\t//\n   517→\t// Min must be greater than or equal to zero,\n   518→\t// and less than or equal to the capacity value.\n   519→\t// requestPolicy.default must be more than or equal to the minimum.\n   520→\t//\n   521→\t// +required\n   522→\tMin *resource.Quantity `json:\"min,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=min\"`\n   523→\n   524→\t// Max defines the upper limit for capacity that can be requested.\n   525→\t//\n   526→\t// Max must be less than or equal to the capacity value.\n   527→\t// Min and requestPolicy.default must be less than or equal to the maximum.\n   528→\t//\n   529→\t// +optional\n   530→\tMax *resource.Quantity `json:\"max,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,opt,name=max\"`\n   531→\n   532→\t// Step defines the step size between valid capacity amounts within the range.\n   533→\t//\n   534→\t// Max (if set) and requestPolicy.default must be a multiple of Step.\n   535→\t// Min + Step must be less than or equal to the capacity value.\n   536→\t//\n   537→\t// +optional\n   538→\tStep *resource.Quantity `json:\"step,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,opt,name=step\"`\n   539→}\n   540→\n   541→// Limit for the sum of the number of entries in both attributes and capacity.\n   542→const ResourceSliceMaxAttributesAndCapacitiesPerDevice = 32\n   543→\n   544→// Limit for the total number of counters in each device.\n   545→const ResourceSliceMaxCountersPerDevice = 32\n   546→\n   547→// Limit for the total number of counters defined in devices in\n   548→// a ResourceSlice. We want to allow up to 64 devices to specify\n   549→// up to 16 counters, so the limit for the ResourceSlice will be 1024.\n   550→const ResourceSliceMaxDeviceCountersPerSlice = 1024 // 64 * 16\n   551→\n   552→// QualifiedName is the name of a device attribute or capacity.\n   553→//\n   554→// Attributes and capacities are defined either by the owner of the specific\n   555→// driver (usually the vendor) or by some 3rd party (e.g. the Kubernetes\n   556→// project). Because they are sometimes compared across devices, a given name\n   557→// is expected to mean the same thing and have the same type on all devices.\n   558→//\n   559→// Names must be either a C identifier (e.g. \"theName\") or a DNS subdomain\n   560→// followed by a slash (\"/\") followed by a C identifier\n   561→// (e.g. \"dra.example.com/theName\"). Names which do not include the\n   562→// domain prefix are assumed to be part of the driver's domain. Attributes\n   563→// or capacities defined by 3rd parties must include the domain prefix.\n   564→//\n   565→// The maximum length for the DNS subdomain is 63 characters (same as\n   566→// for driver names) and the maximum length of the C identifier\n   567→// is 32.\n   568→type QualifiedName string\n   569→\n   570→// FullyQualifiedName is a QualifiedName where the domain is set.\n   571→// Format validation cannot be added to this type because one of its usages,\n   572→// DistinctAttribute, is validated conditionally. This conditional validation\n   573→// cannot be expressed declaratively.\n   574→type FullyQualifiedName string\n   575→\n   576→// DeviceMaxDomainLength is the maximum length of the domain prefix in a fully-qualified name.\n   577→const DeviceMaxDomainLength = 63\n   578→\n   579→// DeviceMaxIDLength is the maximum length of the identifier in a device attribute or capacity name (`<domain>/<ID>`).\n   580→const DeviceMaxIDLength = 32\n   581→\n   582→// DeviceAttribute must have exactly one field set.\n   583→type DeviceAttribute struct {\n   584→\t// The Go field names below have a Value suffix to avoid a conflict between the\n   585→\t// field \"String\" and the corresponding method. That method is required.\n   586→\t// The Kubernetes API is defined without that suffix to keep it more natural.\n   587→\n   588→\t// IntValue is a number.\n   589→\t//\n   590→\t// +optional\n   591→\t// +k8s:optional\n   592→\t// +k8s:unionMember\n   593→\tIntValue *int64 `json:\"int,omitempty\" protobuf:\"varint,2,opt,name=int\"`\n   594→\n   595→\t// BoolValue is a true/false value.\n   596→\t//\n   597→\t// +optional\n   598→\t// +k8s:optional\n   599→\t// +k8s:unionMember\n   600→\tBoolValue *bool `json:\"bool,omitempty\" protobuf:\"varint,3,opt,name=bool\"`\n   601→\n   602→\t// StringValue is a string. It might get added as part of that.\n   655→\n   656→\t// TimeAdded represents the time at which the taint was added.\n   657→\t// Added automatically during create or update if not set.\n   658→\t//\n   659→\t// +optional\n   660→\tTimeAdded *metav1.Time `json:\"timeAdded,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,4,opt,name=timeAdded\"`\n   661→\n   662→\t// ^^^\n   663→\t//\n   664→\t// This field during pod eviction in pkg/controller/tainteviction).\n   667→}\n   668→\n   669→// +enum\n   670→// +k8s:enum\n   671→type DeviceTaintEffect string\n   672→\n   673→const (\n   674→\t// Do not allow With FirstAvailable it is also\n   794→// possible to provide a prioritized list of requests.\n   795→type DeviceRequest struct {\n   796→\t// Name can be used to reference this request in a pod.spec.containers[].resources.claims\n   797→\t// entry and in a constraint of the claim.\n   798→\t//\n   799→\t// References using the name in the DeviceRequest will uniquely\n   800→\t// identify a request when the Exactly field is set. When the\n   801→\t// FirstAvailable field is set, a reference to the name of the\n   802→\t// DeviceRequest will match whatever subrequest is chosen by the\n   803→\t// scheduler.\n   804→\t//\n   805→\t// Must be a DNS label.\n   806→\t//\n   807→\t// +required\n   808→\tName string `json:\"name\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,name=name\"`\n   809→\n   810→\t// Exactly specifies the details for a single request that must\n   811→\t// be met exactly for the request to be satisfied.\n   812→\t//\n   813→\t// One of Exactly or FirstAvailable must be set.\n   814→\t//\n   815→\t// +optional\n   816→\t// +oneOf=deviceRequestType\n   817→\t// +k8s:optional\n   818→\tExactly *ExactDeviceRequest `json:\"exactly,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,name=exactly\"`\n   819→\n   820→\t// FirstAvailable contains subrequests, of which exactly one will be\n   821→\t// selected by the scheduler. It tries to\n   822→\t// satisfy them in the order in which they are listed here. So if\n   823→\t// there are two entries in the list, the scheduler will only check\n   824→\t// the second one if it determines that the first one can not be used.\n   825→\t//\n   826→\t// DRA does not yet implement scoring, so the scheduler will\n   827→\t// select the first set of devices that satisfies all the\n   828→\t// requests in the claim. And if the requirements can\n   829→\t// be satisfied on more than one node, other scheduling features\n   830→\t// will determine which node is chosen. This means that the set of\n   831→\t// devices allocated to a claim might not be the optimal set\n   832→\t// available to the cluster. Scoring will be implemented later.\n   833→\t//\n   834→\t// +optional\n   835→\t// +oneOf=deviceRequestType\n   836→\t// +listType=atomic\n   837→\t// +featureGate=DRAPrioritizedList\n   838→\t// +k8s:listType=atomic\n   839→\t// +k8s:unique=map\n   840→\t// +k8s:listMapKey=name\n   841→\t// +k8s:maxItems=8\n   842→\tFirstAvailable []DeviceSubRequest `json:\"firstAvailable,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,name=firstAvailable\"`\n   843→}\n   844→\n   845→// ExactDeviceRequest is a request for one or more identical devices.\n   846→type ExactDeviceRequest struct {\n   847→\t// DeviceClassName references a specific DeviceClass, which can define\n   848→\t// additional configuration and selectors to be inherited by this\n   849→\t// request.\n   850→\t//\n   851→\t// A DeviceClassName is required.\n   852→\t//\n   853→\t// Administrators may use this to restrict which devices may get\n   854→\t// requested by only installing classes with selectors for permitted\n   855→\t// devices. 1068→\tCapacity *CapacityRequirements `json:\"capacity,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,7,opt,name=capacity\"`\n  1069→}\n  1070→\n  1071→// CapacityRequirements defines the capacity requirements for a specific device request.\n  1072→type CapacityRequirements struct {\n  1073→\t// Requests represent individual device resource requests for distinct resources,\n  1074→\t// all of which must be provided by the device.\n  1075→\t//\n  1076→\t// This value is used as an additional filtering condition against the available capacity on the device.\n  1077→\t// This is semantically equivalent to a CEL selector with\n  1078→\t// `device.capacity[<domain>].<name>.compareTo(quantity(<request quantity>)) >= 0`.\n  1079→\t// For example, device.capacity['test-driver.cdi.k8s.io'].counters.compareTo(quantity('2')) >= 0.\n  1080→\t//\n  1081→\t// When a requestPolicy is defined, the requested amount is adjusted upward\n  1082→\t// to the considered ineligible for allocation.\n  1085→\t//\n  1086→\t// For any capacity that is not explicitly requested:\n  1087→\t// - If no requestPolicy is set, the default consumed capacity is equal to the full device capacity\n  1088→\t//   (i.e., the whole device is claimed).\n  1089→\t// - If a requestPolicy is set, the default consumed capacity is determined according to that policy.\n  1090→\t//\n  1091→\t// If the device allows multiple allocation,\n  1092→\t// the aggregated amount across all requests must not exceed the capacity value.\n  1093→\t// The consumed capacity, which 1124→\n  1125→// CELDeviceSelector contains a CEL expression for selecting a device.\n  1126→type CELDeviceSelector struct {\n  1127→\t// Expression is a CEL expression which evaluates a single device. It\n  1128→\t// must evaluate to true when the device under consideration satisfies\n  1129→\t// the desired criteria, and false when it does not. Any other result\n  1130→\t// is an error and causes allocation of devices to abort.\n  1131→\t//\n  1132→\t// The expression's input is an object named \"device\", which carries\n  1133→\t// the following properties:\n  1134→\t//  - driver (string): the name of the driver which defines this device.\n  1135→\t//  - attributes (map[string]object): the device's attributes, grouped by prefix\n  1136→\t//    (e.g. device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"] evaluates to an object with all\n  1137→\t//    of the attributes which were prefixed by \"dra.example.com\".\n  1138→\t//  - capacity (map[string]object): the device's capacities, grouped by prefix.\n  1139→\t//  - allowMultipleAllocations (bool): the allowMultipleAllocations property of the device\n  1140→\t//    (v1.34+ with the DRAConsumableCapacity feature enabled).\n  1141→\t//\n  1142→\t// Example: Consider a device with driver=\"dra.example.com\", which exposes\n  1143→\t// two attributes named \"model\" and \"ext.example.com/family\" and which\n  1144→\t// exposes one capacity named \"modules\". This input to this expression\n  1145→\t// would have the following fields:\n  1146→\t//\n  1147→\t//     device.driver\n  1148→\t//     device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"].model\n  1149→\t//     device.attributes[\"ext.example.com\"].family\n  1150→\t//     device.capacity[\"dra.example.com\"].modules\n  1151→\t//\n  1152→\t// The device.driver field can be used to check for a specific driver,\n  1153→\t// either as a high-level precondition (i.e. you only want to consider\n  1154→\t// devices from this driver) or as part of a multi-clause expression\n  1155→\t// that is meant to consider devices from different drivers.\n  1156→\t//\n  1157→\t// The value type of each attribute is defined by the device\n  1158→\t// definition, and users who write these expressions must consult the\n  1159→\t// documentation for their specific drivers. The value type of each\n  1160→\t// capacity is Quantity.\n  1161→\t//\n  1162→\t// If an unknown prefix is used as a lookup in either device.attributes\n  1163→\t// or device.capacity, an empty map will be returned. Any reference to\n  1164→\t// an unknown field will cause an evaluation error and allocation to\n  1165→\t// abort.\n  1166→\t//\n  1167→\t// A robust expression should check for the existence of attributes\n  1168→\t// before referencing them.\n  1169→\t//\n  1170→\t// For ease of use, the cel.bind() function is enabled, and can be used\n  1171→\t// to simplify expressions that access multiple attributes with the\n  1172→\t// same domain. For example:\n  1173→\t//\n  1174→\t//     cel.bind(dra, device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"], dra.someBool && dra.anotherBool)\n  1175→\t//\n  1176→\t// The length of the expression must be smaller or equal to 10 Ki. The\n  1177→\t// cost of evaluating it is also limited based on the estimated number\n  1178→\t// of logical steps.\n  1179→\t//\n  1180→\t// +required\n  1181→\tExpression string `json:\"expression\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,name=expression\"`\n  1182→}\n  1183→\n  1184→// CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost specifies the cost limit for a single CEL selector\n  1185→// evaluation.\n  1186→//\n  1187→// There is no overall budget for selecting a device, so the actual time\n  1188→// required for that is proportional to the number of CEL selectors and how\n  1189→// often they need to be evaluated, which can vary depending on several factors\n  1190→// (number of devices, cluster utilization, additional constraints).\n  1191→//\n  1192→// Validation against this limit and [CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength] happens\n  1193→// only when setting an expression for the first time or when changing it. If\n  1194→// the limits are changed in a future Kubernetes release, existing users are\n  1195→// guaranteed that existing expressions will continue to be valid.\n  1196→//\n  1197→// However, the kube-scheduler also applies this cost limit at runtime, so it\n  1198→// could happen that a valid expression fails at runtime after an up- or\n  1199→// downgrade. This can also happen without version skew when the cost estimate\n  1200→// underestimated the actual cost. That this might happen is the reason why\n  1201→// kube-scheduler enforces the runtime limit instead of relying on validation.\n  1202→//\n  1203→// According to\n  1204→// https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/blob/4aeaf1e99e82da8334c0d6dddd848a194cd44b4f/staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/apis/cel/config.go#L20-L22,\n  1205→// this gives roughly 0.1 second for each expression evaluation.\n  1206→// However, this depends on how fast the machine is.\n  1207→const CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost = 1000000\n  1208→\n  1209→// CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength is the maximum length of a CEL selector expression string.\n  1210→const CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength = 10 * 1024\n  1211→\n  1212→// DeviceConstraint must have exactly one field set besides Requests.\n  1213→type DeviceConstraint struct {\n  1214→\t// Requests is a list of the one or more requests in this claim which\n  1215→\t// must co-satisfy this constraint. A claim that is in\n  1409→\t// use or might be in use because it has been reserved must not get\n  1410→\t// deallocated.\n  1411→\t//\n  1412→\t// In a cluster with multiple scheduler instances, two pods might get\n  1413→\t// scheduled concurrently by different schedulers. field.ErrorList {\n   317→\tvar allErrs field.ErrorList\n   318→\tenvType := environment.NewExpressions\n   319→\tif stored {\n   320→\t\tenvType = environment.StoredExpressions\n   321→\t}\n   322→\tif len(celSelector.Expression) > resource.CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength {\n   323→\t\tallErrs = append(allErrs, field.TooLong(fldPath.Child(\"expression\"), \"\" /*unused*/, resource.CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength))\n   324→\t\t// Don't bother compiling too long expressions.\n   325→\t\treturn allErrs\n   326→\t}\n   327→\n   328→\tresult := dracel.GetCompiler(dracel.Features{EnableConsumableCapacity: utilfeature.DefaultFeatureGate.Enabled(features.DRAConsumableCapacity)}).CompileCELExpression(celSelector.Expression, dracel.Options{EnvType: &envType})\n   329→\tif result.Error != nil {\n   330→\t\tallErrs = append(allErrs, convertCELErrorToValidationError(fldPath.Child(\"expression\"), celSelector.Expression, result.Error))\n   331→\t} else if result.MaxCost > resource.CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost {\n   332→\t\tallErrs = append(allErrs, field.Forbidden(fldPath.Child(\"expression\"), \"too complex, exceeds cost limit\"))\n   333→\t}\n   334→\n   335→\treturn allErrs\n   336→}\n   337→\n   338→func convertCELErrorToValidationError(fldPath *field.Path, expression string, err error) *field.Error {\n   339→\tvar celErr *cel.Error\n   340→\tif errors.As(err, &celErr) {\n   341→\t\tswitch celErr.Type {\n   342→\t\tcase cel.ErrorTypeRequired:\n   343→\t\t\treturn field.Required(fldPath, celErr.Detail)\n   344→\t\tcase cel.ErrorTypeInvalid:\n   345→\t\t\treturn field.Invalid(fldPath, expression, celErr.Detail)\n   346→\t\tcase cel.ErrorTypeInternal:\n   347→\t\t\treturn field.InternalError(fldPath, celErr)\n   348→\t\t}\n   349→\t}\n   350→\treturn field.InternalError(fldPath, fmt.Errorf(\"unsupported error type: %w\", err))\n   351→}\n   352→\n   353→func bool, requestNames requestNames, fldPath *field.Path) field.ErrorList {\n   418→\tvar allErrs field.ErrorList\n   419→\tallErrs = append(allErrs, validateSet(status.ReservedFor, resource.ResourceClaimReservedForMaxSize,\n   420→\t\tvalidateResourceClaimUserReference,\n   421→\t\tfunc(consumer resource.ResourceClaimConsumerReference) types.UID { return consumer.UID },\n not be validated again because\n   459→\t// validation for new results is tighter than it was before.\n   460→\tif oldStatus.Allocation != nil && status.Allocation != nil {\n   461→\t\tallErrs = option.Cmp(maxCapacity) > 0 {\n  1022→\t\t\t\tallErrs = append(allErrs, field.Invalid(fldPath, option.String(), fmt.Sprintf(\"option is larger than capacity value: %s\", maxCapacity.String())))\n  1023→\t\t\t}\n  1024→\t\t\tif option.Cmp(defaultValue) == 0 {\n  1025→\t\t\t\tfoundDefault = true\n  1026→\t\t\t}\n  1027→\t\t\treturn allErrs\n  1028→\t\t}, quantityKey, fldPath)...)\n  1029→\tif !foundDefault {\n  1030→\t\tallErrs = append(allErrs, field.Invalid(fldPath, defaultValue.String(), \"default value is not valid according to the requestPolicy\"))\n  1031→\t}\n  1032→\treturn allErrs\n  1033→}\n  1034→\n  1035→func validateRequestPolicyRange(defaultValue apiresource.Quantity, maxCapacity apiresource.Quantity, valueRange resource.CapacityRequestPolicyRange, fldPath *field.Path) field.ErrorList {\n  1036→\tvar allErrs field.ErrorList\n  1037→\tif valueRange.Min == nil {\n  1038→\t\tallErrs = append(allErrs, field.Required(fldPath.Child(\"min\"), \"required when
