Whenever a driver\n   226→\t// changes something about one or more of the resources in a pool, it\n   227→\t// must change the generation in all ResourceSlices which are part of\n   228→\t// that pool. Consumers of ResourceSlices should only consider\n   229→\t// resources from the pool with the highest generation number. The\n   230→\t// generation may be reset by drivers, which should be fine for\n   231→\t// consumers, assuming that all ResourceSlices in a pool are updated to\n   232→\t// match or deleted.\n   233→\t//\n   234→\t// Combined with ResourceSliceCount, this mechanism enables consumers to\n   235→\t// detect pools which are comprised of multiple ResourceSlices and are\n   236→\t// in an incomplete state.\n   237→\t//\n   238→\t// +required\n   239→\tGeneration int64 `json:\"generation\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,name=generation\"`\n   240→\n   241→\t// ResourceSliceCount is the total number of ResourceSlices in the pool at this\n   242→\t// generation number. Must be greater than zero.\n   243→\t//\n   244→\t// Consumers can use this to check whether they have seen all ResourceSlices\n   245→\t// belonging to the same pool.\n   246→\t//\n   247→\t// +required\n   248→\tResourceSliceCount int64 `json:\"resourceSliceCount\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,name=resourceSliceCount\"`\n   249→}\n   250→\n   251→const ResourceSliceMaxSharedCapacity = 128\n   252→const ResourceSliceMaxDevices = 128\n   253→const PoolNameMaxLength = validation.DNS1123SubdomainMaxLength // Same as for a single node name.\n   254→const BindingConditionsMaxSize = 4\n   255→const BindingFailureConditionsMaxSize = 4\n   256→\n   257→// Defines the max number of shared counters that can be specified\n   258→// in a ResourceSlice. With FirstAvailable it is also\n   794→// possible to provide a prioritized list of requests.\n   795→type DeviceRequest struct {\n   796→\t// Name can be used to reference this request in a pod.spec.containers[].resources.claims\n   797→\t// entry and in a constraint of the claim.\n   798→\t//\n   799→\t// References using the name in the DeviceRequest will uniquely\n   800→\t// identify a request when the Exactly field is set. When the\n   801→\t// FirstAvailable field is set, a reference to the name of the\n   802→\t// DeviceRequest will match whatever subrequest is chosen by the\n   803→\t// scheduler.\n   804→\t//\n   805→\t// Must be a DNS label.\n   806→\t//\n   807→\t// +required\n   808→\tName string `json:\"name\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,name=name\"`\n   809→\n   810→\t// Exactly specifies the details for a single request that must\n   811→\t// be met exactly for the request to be satisfied.\n   812→\t//\n   813→\t// One of Exactly or FirstAvailable must be set.\n   814→\t//\n   815→\t// +optional\n   816→\t// +oneOf=deviceRequestType\n   817→\t// +k8s:optional\n   818→\tExactly *ExactDeviceRequest `json:\"exactly,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,name=exactly\"`\n   819→\n   820→\t// FirstAvailable contains subrequests, of which exactly one will be\n   821→\t// selected by the scheduler. It tries to\n   822→\t// satisfy them in the order in which they are listed here. So if\n   823→\t// there are two entries in the list, the scheduler will only check\n   824→\t// the second one if it determines that the first one can not be used.\n   825→\t//\n   826→\t// DRA does not yet implement scoring, so the scheduler will\n   827→\t// select the first set of devices that satisfies all the\n   828→\t// requests in the claim. And if the requirements can\n   829→\t// be satisfied on more than one node, other scheduling features\n   830→\t// will determine which node is chosen. This means that the set of\n   831→\t// devices allocated to a claim might not be the optimal set\n   832→\t// available to the cluster. Scoring will be implemented later.\n   833→\t//\n   834→\t// +optional\n   835→\t// +oneOf=deviceRequestType\n   836→\t// +listType=atomic\n   837→\t// +featureGate=DRAPrioritizedList\n   838→\t// +k8s:listType=atomic\n   839→\t// +k8s:unique=map\n   840→\t// +k8s:listMapKey=name\n   841→\t// +k8s:maxItems=8\n   842→\tFirstAvailable []DeviceSubRequest `json:\"firstAvailable,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,name=firstAvailable\"`\n   843→}\n   844→\n   845→// ExactDeviceRequest is a request for one or more identical devices.\n   846→type ExactDeviceRequest struct {\n   847→\t// DeviceClassName references a specific DeviceClass, which can define\n   848→\t// additional configuration and selectors to be inherited by this\n   849→\t// request.\n   850→\t//\n   851→\t// A DeviceClassName is required.\n   852→\t//\n   853→\t// Administrators may use this to restrict which devices may get\n   854→\t// requested by only installing classes with selectors for permitted\n   855→\t// devices. The exact number is provided in the\n   877→\t//   count field.\n   878→\t//\n   879→\t// - All: This request is for all of the matching devices in a pool.\n   880→\t//   At least one device must exist on the node for the allocation to succeed.\n   881→\t//   Allocation will fail if some devices are already allocated,\n   882→\t//   unless adminAccess is requested.\n   883→\t//\n   884→\t// If AllocationMode is not specified, the default mode is ExactCount. The same applies\n   920→\t// to NoExecute.\n   921→\t//\n   922→\t// In addition, should any of the allocated devices get tainted\n   923→\t// with NoExecute after allocation and that effect is not tolerated,\n   924→\t// then all pods consuming the ResourceClaim get deleted to evict\n   925→\t// them. specific device request.\n  1072→type CapacityRequirements struct {\n  1073→\t// Requests represent individual device resource requests for distinct resources,\n  1074→\t// all of which must be provided by the device.\n  1075→\t//\n  1076→\t// This value is used as an additional filtering condition against the available capacity on the device.\n  1077→\t// This is semantically equivalent to a CEL selector with\n  1078→\t// `device.capacity[<domain>].<name>.compareTo(quantity(<request quantity>)) >= 0`.\n  1079→\t// For example, device.capacity['test-driver.cdi.k8s.io'].counters.compareTo(quantity('2')) >= 0.\n  1080→\t//\n  1081→\t// When a requestPolicy is defined, the requested amount is adjusted upward\n  1082→\t// to the nearest valid value based on the policy.\n  1083→\t// If the requested amount cannot be adjusted to a valid value—because it exceeds what the requestPolicy allows—\n  1084→\t// the device is considered ineligible for allocation.\n  1085→\t//\n  1086→\t// For any capacity that is not explicitly requested:\n  1087→\t// - If no requestPolicy is set, the default consumed capacity is equal to the full device capacity\n  1088→\t//   (i.e., the whole device is claimed).\n  1089→\t// - If a requestPolicy is set, the default consumed capacity is determined according to that policy.\n  1090→\t//\n  1091→\t// If the device allows multiple allocation,\n  1092→\t// the aggregated amount across all requests must not exceed the capacity value.\n  1093→\t// The consumed capacity, which may be adjusted based on the requestPolicy if defined,\n  1094→\t// is recorded in the resource claim’s status.devices[*].consumedCapacity field.\n  1095→\t//\n  1096→\t// +optional\n  1097→\tRequests map[QualifiedName]resource.Quantity `json:\"requests,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,rep,name=requests,castkey=QualifiedName\"`\n  1098→}\n  1099→\n  1100→const (\n  1101→\tDeviceSelectorsMaxSize             = 32\n  1102→\tFirstAvailableDeviceRequestMaxSize = 8\n  1103→\tDeviceTolerationsMaxLength         = 16\n  1104→)\n  1105→\n  1106→// +enum\n  1107→// +k8s:enum\n  1108→type DeviceAllocationMode string\n  1109→\n  1110→// Valid [DeviceRequest.CountMode] values.\n  1111→const (\n  1112→\tDeviceAllocationModeExactCount = DeviceAllocationMode(\"ExactCount\")\n  1113→\tDeviceAllocationModeAll        = DeviceAllocationMode(\"All\")\n  1114→)\n  1115→\n  1116→// DeviceSelector must have exactly one field set.\n  1117→type DeviceSelector struct {\n  1118→\t// CEL contains a CEL expression for selecting a device.\n  1119→\t//\n  1120→\t// +optional\n  1121→\t// +oneOf=SelectorType\n  1122→\tCEL *CELDeviceSelector `json:\"cel,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=cel\"`\n  1123→}\n  1124→\n  1125→// CELDeviceSelector contains a CEL expression for selecting a device.\n  1126→type CELDeviceSelector struct {\n  1127→\t// Expression is a CEL expression which evaluates a single device. It\n  1128→\t// must evaluate to true when the device under consideration satisfies\n  1129→\t// the desired criteria, and false when it does not. Any other result\n  1130→\t// is an error and causes allocation of devices to abort.\n  1131→\t//\n  1132→\t// The expression's input is an object named \"device\", which carries\n  1133→\t// the following properties:\n  1134→\t//  - driver (string): the name of the driver which defines this device.\n  1135→\t//  - attributes (map[string]object): the device's attributes, grouped by prefix\n  1136→\t//    (e.g. device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"] evaluates to an object with all\n  1137→\t//    of the attributes which were prefixed by \"dra.example.com\".\n  1138→\t//  - capacity (map[string]object): the device's capacities, grouped by prefix.\n  1139→\t//  - allowMultipleAllocations (bool): the allowMultipleAllocations property of the device\n  1140→\t//    (v1.34+ with the DRAConsumableCapacity feature enabled).\n  1141→\t//\n  1142→\t// Example: Consider a device with driver=\"dra.example.com\", which exposes\n  1143→\t// two attributes named \"model\" and \"ext.example.com/family\" and which\n  1144→\t// exposes one capacity named \"modules\". This input to this expression\n  1145→\t// would have the following fields:\n  1146→\t//\n  1147→\t//     device.driver\n  1148→\t//     device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"].model\n  1149→\t//     device.attributes[\"ext.example.com\"].family\n  1150→\t//     device.capacity[\"dra.example.com\"].modules\n  1151→\t//\n  1152→\t// The device.driver field can be used to check for a specific driver,\n  1153→\t// either as a high-level precondition (i.e. you only want to consider\n  1154→\t// devices from this driver) or as part of a multi-clause expression\n  1155→\t// that is meant to consider devices from different drivers.\n  1156→\t//\n  1157→\t// The value type of each attribute is defined by the device\n  1158→\t// definition, and users who write these expressions must consult the\n  1159→\t// documentation for their specific drivers. The value type of each\n  1160→\t// capacity is Quantity.\n  1161→\t//\n  1162→\t// If an unknown prefix is used as a lookup in either device.attributes\n  1163→\t// or device.capacity, an empty map will be returned. Any reference to\n  1164→\t// an unknown field will cause an evaluation error and allocation to\n  1165→\t// abort.\n  1166→\t//\n  1167→\t// A robust expression should check for the existence of attributes\n  1168→\t// before referencing them.\n  1169→\t//\n  1170→\t// For ease of use, the cel.bind() function is enabled, and can be used\n  1171→\t// to simplify expressions that access multiple attributes with the\n  1172→\t// same domain. For example:\n  1173→\t//\n  1174→\t//     cel.bind(dra, device.attributes[\"dra.example.com\"], dra.someBool && dra.anotherBool)\n  1175→\t//\n  1176→\t// The length of the expression must be smaller or equal to 10 Ki. The\n  1177→\t// cost of evaluating it is also limited based on the estimated number\n  1178→\t// of logical steps.\n  1179→\t//\n  1180→\t// +required\n  1181→\tExpression string `json:\"expression\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,name=expression\"`\n  1182→}\n  1183→\n  1184→// CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost specifies the cost limit for a single CEL selector\n  1185→// evaluation.\n  1186→//\n  1187→// There is no overall budget for selecting a device, so the actual time\n  1188→// required for that is proportional to the number of CEL selectors and how\n  1189→// often they need to be evaluated, which can vary depending on several factors\n  1190→// (number of devices, cluster utilization, additional constraints).\n  1191→//\n  1192→// Validation against this limit and [CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength] happens\n  1193→// only when setting an expression for the first time or when changing it. If\n  1194→// the limits are changed in a future Kubernetes release, existing users are\n  1195→// guaranteed that existing expressions will continue to be valid.\n  1196→//\n  1197→// However, the kube-scheduler also applies this cost limit at runtime, so it\n  1198→// could happen that a valid expression fails at runtime after an up- or\n  1199→// downgrade. This can also happen without version skew when the cost estimate\n  1200→// underestimated the actual cost. That this might happen is the reason why\n  1201→// kube-scheduler enforces the runtime limit instead of relying on validation.\n  1202→//\n  1203→// According to\n  1204→// https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/blob/4aeaf1e99e82da8334c0d6dddd848a194cd44b4f/staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/apis/cel/config.go#L20-L22,\n  1205→// this gives roughly 0.1 second for each expression evaluation.\n  1206→// However, this depends on how fast the machine is.\n  1207→const CELSelectorExpressionMaxCost = 1000000\n  1208→\n  1209→// CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength is the maximum length of a CEL selector expression string.\n  1210→const CELSelectorExpressionMaxLength = 10 * 1024\n  1211→\n  1212→// DeviceConstraint must have exactly one field set besides Requests.\n  1213→type DeviceConstraint struct {\n  1214→\t// Requests is a list of the one or more requests in this claim which\n  1215→\t// must co-satisfy this constraint. If a request is fulfilled by\n  1216→\t// multiple devices, then all of the devices must satisfy the\n  1217→\t// constraint. If this is not specified, this constraint applies to all\n  1218→\t// requests in this claim.\n  1219→\t//\n  1220→\t// References to subrequests must include the name of the main request\n  1221→\t// and may include the subrequest using the format <main request>[/<subrequest>]. If just\n  1222→\t// the main request is given, the constraint applies to all subrequests.\n  1223→\t//\n  1224→\t// +optional\n  1225→\t// +listType=atomic\n  1226→\t// +k8s:listType=atomic\n  1227→\t// +k8s:unique=set\n  1228→\t// +k8s:maxItems=32\n  1229→\tRequests []string `json:\"requests,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=requests\"`\n  1230→\n  1231→\t// MatchAttribute requires that all devices in question have this\n  1232→\t// attribute and that its type and value are the same across those\n  1233→\t// devices.\n  1234→\t//\n  1235→\t// For example, if you specified \"dra.example.com/numa\" (a hypothetical example!),\n  1236→\t// then only devices in the same NUMA node will be chosen. A device which\n  1237→\t// does not have that attribute will not be chosen. All devices should\n  1238→\t// use a value of the same type for this attribute because that is part of\n  1239→\t// its specification, but if one device doesn't, then it also will not be\n  1240→\t// chosen.\n  1241→\t//\n  1242→\t// Must include the domain qualifier.\n  1243→\t//\n  1244→\t// +optional\n  1245→\t// +oneOf=ConstraintType\n  1246→\t// +k8s:optional\n  1247→\t// +k8s:format=k8s-resource-fully-qualified-name\n  1248→\tMatchAttribute *FullyQualifiedName `json:\"matchAttribute,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,2,opt,name=matchAttribute\"`\n  1249→\n  1250→\t// Potential future extension, not part of the current design:\n  1251→\t// A CEL expression which compares different devices and returns\n  1252→\t// true if they match.\n  1253→\t//\n  1254→\t// Because it would be part of a one-of, old schedulers will not\n  1255→\t// accidentally ignore this additional, for them unknown match\n  1256→\t// criteria.\n  1257→\t//\n  1258→\t// MatchExpression string\n  1259→\n  1260→\t// DistinctAttribute requires that all devices in question have this\n  1261→\t// attribute and that its type and value are unique across those devices.\n  1262→\t//\n  1263→\t// This acts as the inverse of MatchAttribute.\n  1264→\t//\n  1265→\t// This constraint is used to avoid allocating multiple requests to the same device\n  1266→\t// by ensuring attribute-level differentiation.\n  1267→\t//\n  1268→\t// This is useful for scenarios where resource requests must be fulfilled by separate physical devices.\n  1269→\t// For example, a container requests two network interfaces that must be allocated from two different physical NICs.\n  1270→\t//\n  1271→\t// +optional\n  1272→\t// +oneOf=ConstraintType\n  1273→\t// +featureGate=DRAConsumableCapacity\n  1274→\tDistinctAttribute *FullyQualifiedName `json:\"distinctAttribute,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,3,opt,name=distinctAttribute\"`\n  1275→}\n  1276→\n  1277→// Empty means match all taint keys.\n  1344→\t// If the key is empty, operator must be Exists; this combination means to match all values and all keys.\n  1345→\t// Must be a label name.\n  1346→\t//\n  1347→\t// +optional\n  1348→\t// +k8s:optional\n  1349→\t// +k8s:format=k8s-label-key\n  1350→\tKey string `json:\"key,omitempty\" protobuf:\"bytes,1,opt,name=key\"`\n  1351→\n  1352→\t// Operator represents a key's relationship to the value.\n  1353→\t// Valid operators are Exists and Equal. A Pod which references a ResourceClaim which is not\n  1408→\t// reserved for that Pod will not be started. A claim that is in\n  1409→\t// use or might be in use because it has been reserved must not get\n  1410→\t// deallocated.\n  1411→\t//\n  1412→\t// In a cluster with multiple scheduler instances, two pods might get\n  1413→\t// scheduled concurrently by different schedulers. When they reference\n  1414→\t// the same ResourceClaim which already has reached its maximum number\n  1415→\t// of consumers, only one pod can be scheduled.\n  1416→\t//\n  1417→\t// Both schedulers try to add their pod to the claim.status.reservedFor\n  1418→\t// field, but only the update that reaches the API server first gets\n  1419→\t// stored. Calls `state.allocator.Allocate()` to attempt device allocation per node\n  - Caches results in `nodeAllocations` map (keyed by node name)\n  - Returns Unschedulable if any claims can't be allocated on the node\n\n- **Reserve Phase** (`dynamicresources.go:1085-1181`):\n  - `count` field MUST be 0 (line 272-274)\n   - ONLY devices in single-node pools can be allocated this way (enforced in allocator, not validation)\n   - All matching devices in pool must be available\n\n2. **For DeviceAllocationModeExactCount**:\n   - `count` field MUST be > 0 (line 276-278)\n   - Allocates exactly N matching devices\n\n#### **3.2 Where Validation Happens**\n\n- **API Server** (`ValidateResourceClaim` - line 109): Called on create/update\n- **Validation Webhook** (optional): CRD validation rules (in `zz_generated.validations.go`)\n- **Scheduler** (Filter phase): No additional validation, trusts API server\n\n#### **3.3 Multi-Node Restriction Status**\n\n**Current State (as of HEAD)**:\n- NO validation rule explicitly prevents AllocationMode.All from multi-node pools at API level\n- Restriction is **enforced in allocator logic** (in structured/internal/*/pools_*.go)\n- **WAS LIFTED** in commit 2e534d6da10 but **REVERTED** in a0e500ace39\n\n**Allocator-Level Enforcement**:\nThe allocator marks pools as \"incomplete\" if they span multiple nodes, and AllocationMode.All rejects incomplete pools (this logic was modified but reverted).\n\n---\n\n### 4. RESOURCE CLAIM AND DEVICE REQUEST FLOW\n\n#### **4.1 Pod to Scheduler Pipeline**\n\n```\nPod.Spec.ResourceClaims[] \n    ↓\n[DynamicResourceAllocation admission controller]\n    ↓ (creates   ↓ (marks as \"in-flight\")\n[Scheduler PreBind: pl.bindClaim()]\n    ↓ (updates claim status)\nResourceClaim.Status.ReservedFor[] + Allocation\n    ↓\n[Kubelet: UpdatePodFromAllocation()]\n    ↓ (reads allocation)\nPod.Spec.Containers[].Resources.Allocated\n```\n\n#### **4.2 Device Request Structure**\n\n```go\ntype DeviceRequest struct {\n    Name string\n    Exactly *ExactDeviceRequest  // One of these two\n    FirstAvailable []DeviceSubRequest\n}\n\ntype ExactDeviceRequest struct {\n    DeviceClassName string\n    Selectors []DeviceSelector  // CEL expressions\n    AllocationMode DeviceAllocationMode  // \"All\" or \"ExactCount\"\n    Count int64  // Only for ExactCount\n    AdminAccess *bool\n    Tolerations []DeviceToleration\n}\n```\n\n#### **4.3 Allocation Result map[QualifiedName]resource.Quantity\n}\n```\n\n**Allocation Flow**:\n1. Allocator selects devices matching selectors\n2. For AllocationMode.All: allocates ALL matching devices on target node\n3. For AllocationMode.ExactCount: allocates exactly N matching devices\n4. Returns NodeSelector restricting claim usage to allocated node\n5. Kubelet reads allocation and configures container\n\n---\n\n### 5. KEY FINDINGS AND IMPLICATIONS\n\n#### **Major Insight: AllocationMode.All and Multi-Node Pools**\n\nThe commit history reveals a **known limitation**:\n\n1. **Original Design**: AllocationMode.All only for single-node pools\n   - Simpler allocation logic\n   - No performance overhead\n\n2. **Expansion Attempt (2e534d6da10)**:\n   - Tried to support multi-node pools with AllocationMode.All\n   - Required global pool state tracking across nodes\n   - **Caused performance regression** (allocating on every node becomes expensive)\n\n3. **Current Status (a0e500ace39 - HEAD)**:\n   - Reverted the change\n   - Back to single-node-only restriction\n   - \"A different fix is needed\"\n   - Indicates this is an active development area\n\n#### **Performance-Critical Path: Pool Gathering**\n\nThe **GatherPools()** function in allocator is critical:\n- Called during Filter phase for every pod-node pair\n- Must determine which devices are available on the node\n- For multi-node pools with AllocationMode.All, needs to know the global pool state\n- Previous optimization attempted to track this globally, but it caused regression\n\n#### **No API-Level Multi-Node Restriction**\n\n**Important**: There is **NO API validation** preventing AllocationMode.All on multi-node pools. The restriction is **algorithm-level** (allocator rejects incomplete pools for AllocationMode.All).\n\nThis means:\n- Users CAN create ResourceClaims with AllocationMode.All on multi-node pools\n- Scheduler will reject allocation as \"incomplete pool\"\n- Pod becomes unschedulable\n\n---\n\n### 6. For each individual device we pick one possible\n   127→\t// candidate after the other, checking constraints as we go.\n   128→\t// Each chosen candidate is marked as \"in use\" and the process\n   129→\t// continues, recursively. This way, all requests get matched against\n   130→\t// all candidates in all possible orders.\n   131→\t//\n   132→\t// The first full solution is chosen.\n   133→\t//\n   134→\t// In other words, this is an exhaustive search. This is okay because\n   135→\t// it aborts early. Once scoring gets added, more intelligence may be\n   136→\t// needed to avoid trying \"equivalent\" solutions (two identical\n   137→\t// requests, two identical devices, two solutions that are the same in\n   138→\t// practice).\n   139→\n   140→\t// This is where we sanity check that we can actually handle the claims\n   141→\t// and their requests. slices.Contains(config.Requests, subRequestName) {\n   339→\t\t\t\t\tallocationResult.Devices.Config = append(allocationResult.Devices.Config, resourceapi.DeviceAllocationConfiguration{\n   340→\t\t\t\t\t\tSource:              resourceapi.AllocationConfigSourceClaim,\n   341→\t\t\t\t\t\tRequests:            config.Requests,\n   342→\t\t\t\t\t\tDeviceConfiguration: config.DeviceConfiguration,\n   343→\t\t\t\t\t})\n   344→\t\t\t\t}\n   345→\t\t\t}\n   346→\t\t}\n   347→\n   348→\t\t// Determine node selector.\n   349→\t\tnodeSelector, err := alloc.createNodeSelector(internalResult.devices)\n   350→\t\tif err != nil {\n   351→\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create NodeSelector for claim %s: %w\", claim.Name, err)\n   352→\t\t}\n   353→\t\tallocationResult.NodeSelector = nodeSelector\n   354→\t}\n   355→\n   356→\treturn result, nil\n   357→}\n   358→\n   359→func (a *Allocator) GetStats() *Stats {\n   360→\t// Not implemented.\n   361→\treturn nil\n   362→}\n   363→\n   364→func (alloc *allocator) validateDeviceRequest(request requestAccessor, parentRequest requestAccessor, requestKey requestIndices, pools []*Pool) (requestData, error) {\n   365→\tclaim := alloc.claimsToAllocate[requestKey.claimIndex]\n   366→\trequestData := requestData{\n   367→\t\trequest:       request,\n   368→\t\tparentRequest: parentRequest,\n   369→\t}\n   370→\tfor i, selector := range request.selectors() {\n   371→\t\tif selector.CEL == nil {\n   372→\t\t\t// Unknown future selector type!\n   373→\t\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s, selector #%d: CEL expression empty (unsupported selector type?)\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), i)\n   374→\t\t}\n   375→\t}\n   376→\n   377→\tif !alloc.features.AdminAccess && request.hasAdminAccess() {\n   378→\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: admin access is requested, but the feature is disabled\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name())\n   379→\t}\n   380→\n   381→\t// Should be set. If it isn't, something changed and we should refuse to proceed.\n   382→\tif request.deviceClassName() == \"\" {\n   383→\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: missing device class name (unsupported request type?)\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name())\n   384→\t}\n   385→\tclass, err := alloc.classLister.Get(request.deviceClassName())\n   386→\tif err != nil {\n   387→\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: could not retrieve device class %s: %w\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), request.deviceClassName(), err)\n   388→\t}\n   389→\n   390→\t// Start collecting information about the request.\n   391→\t// The class must be set and stored before calling isSelectable.\n   392→\trequestData.class = class\n   393→\n   394→\tswitch request.allocationMode() {\n   395→\tcase resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeExactCount:\n   396→\t\tnumDevices := request.count()\n   397→\t\tif numDevices > math.MaxInt {\n   398→\t\t\t// Allowed by API validation, but doesn't make sense.\n   399→\t\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: exact count %d is too large\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), numDevices)\n   400→\t\t}\n   401→\t\trequestData.numDevices = int(numDevices)\n   402→\tcase resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeAll:\n   403→\t\t// If we have any any request that wants \"all\" devices, we need to\n   404→\t\t// figure out how much \"all\" is. If some pool is incomplete, we stop\n   405→\t\t// here because allocation cannot succeed. Once we do scoring, we should\n   406→\t\t// stop in all cases, not just when \"all\" devices are needed, because\n   407→\t\t// pulling from an incomplete might not pick the best solution and it's\n   408→\t\t// better to wait. This does not matter yet as long the incomplete pool\n   409→\t\t// has some matching device.\n   410→\t\trequestData.allDevices = make([]deviceWithID, 0, resourceapi.AllocationResultsMaxSize)\n   411→\t\tfor _, pool := range pools {\n   412→\t\t\tif pool.IsIncomplete {\n   413→\t\t\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently being updated\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), pool.PoolID)\n   414→\t\t\t}\n   415→\t\t\tif pool.IsInvalid {\n   416→\t\t\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently invalid\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), pool.PoolID)\n   417→\t\t\t}\n   418→\n   419→\t\t\tfor _, slice := range pool.Slices {\n   420→\t\t\t\tfor 423→\t\t\t\t\t\treturn requestData, err\n   424→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   425→\t\t\t\t\tif selectable {\n   426→\t\t\t\t\t\tdevice := deviceWithID{\n   427→\t\t\t\t\t\t\tid:     DeviceID{Driver: 430→\t\t\t\t\t\t}\n   431→\t\t\t\t\t\trequestData.allDevices = append(requestData.allDevices, device)\n   432→\t\t\t\t\t}\n   433→\t\t\t\t}\n   434→\t\t\t}\n   435→\t\t}\n   436→\t\trequestData.numDevices = len(requestData.allDevices)\n   437→\t\talloc.logger.V(6).Info(\"Request for 'all' devices\", \"claim\", klog.KObj(claim), \"request\", request.name(), request.allocationMode())\n   440→\t}\n   441→\treturn requestData, nil\n   442→}\n   443→\n   444→// errStop is a special error that gets returned by allocateOne if it detects\n   445→// that allocation cannot succeed.\n   446→var errStop = errors.New(\"stop allocation\")\n   447→\n   448→// allocator is used while an [Allocator.Allocate] is running. Only a single\n   449→// goroutine works with it, so there is no need for locking.\n   450→type allocator struct {\n   451→\t*Allocator\n   452→\tctx                  context.Context\n   453→\tlogger               klog.Logger\n   454→\tnode                 *v1.Node\n   455→\tclaimsToAllocate     []*resourceapi.ResourceClaim\n   456→\tpools                []*Pool\n   457→\tdeviceMatchesRequest map[matchKey]bool\n   458→\tconstraints          [][]constraint // one list of constraints per claim\n   459→\t// consumedCounters keeps track of the counters consumed by all devices\n   460→\t// that are in the process of being allocated.\n   461→\t// The keys in the map are ResourceSlice names.\n   462→\tconsumedCounters map[string]counterSets\n   463→\trequestData      map[requestIndices]requestData // one entry per request with no subrequests and one entry per subrequest\n   464→\t// allocatingDevices tracks which devices will be newly allocated for a\n   465→\t// particular attempt to find a solution. The map is indexed by device\n   466→\t// and its values represent for which of a pod's claims the device will\n   467→\t// be allocated.\n   468→\t// Claims are identified by their index in claimsToAllocate.\n   469→\tallocatingDevices map[DeviceID]sets.Set[int]\n   470→\tresult            []internalAllocationResult\n   471→}\n   472→\n   473→// counterSets is a map with the name of counter sets to the counters in\n   474→// the set.\n   475→type counterSets map[draapi.UniqueString]map[string]resourceapi.Counter\n   476→\n   477→// matchKey identifies a device/request pair.\n   478→type matchKey struct {\n   479→\tDeviceID\n   480→\trequestIndices\n   481→}\n   482→\n   483→// requestIndices identifies one specific request\n   484→// or subrequest by three properties:\n   485→//\n   486→// - claimIndex: The index of the claim in the requestData map.\n   487→// - requestIndex: The index of the request in the claim.\n   488→// - subRequestIndex: The index of the subrequest in the parent request.\n   489→type requestIndices struct {\n   490→\tclaimIndex, requestIndex int\n   491→\tsubRequestIndex          int\n   492→}\n   493→\n   494→// deviceIndices identifies one specific required device inside\n   495→// a request or subrequest of a certain claim.\n   496→type deviceIndices struct {\n   497→\tclaimIndex      int // The index of the claim in the allocator.\n   498→\trequestIndex    int // The index of the request in the claim.\n   499→\tsubRequestIndex int // The index of the subrequest within the request (ignored if subRequest is false).\n   500→\tdeviceIndex     int // The index of a device within a request or subrequest.\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. DeviceClassLister = internal.DeviceClassLister\ntype Features = internal.Features\ntype DeviceID = internal.DeviceID\ntype Stats = internal.Stats\n\nfunc MakeDeviceID(driver, pool, device string) DeviceID {\n\treturn internal.MakeDeviceID(driver, pool, device)\n}\n\n// SupportedFeatures does not include any additional features.\n// The \"stable\" implementation can only be used if all of internal.Features{}\n\ntype Allocator struct {\n\tfeatures         Features\n\tallocatedDevices sets.Set[DeviceID]\n\tclassLister      DeviceClassLister\n\tslices           []*resourceapi.ResourceSlice\n\tcelCache         *cel.Cache\n\t// availableCounters contains the available counters for individual\n\t// ResourceSlices. It acts as a cache that is updated the first time\n\t// the available counters are needed for each ResourceSlice. The information\n\t// about each slice is never updated once set the first time.\n\t// This is computed bsed on information on the Allocator, so it will\n\t// be correct even for multiple usages of the Allocator.\n\t// The keys in the map are ResourceSlice names.\n\t// The allocator might be accessed by different goroutines, so\n\t// access to this map must be synchronized.\n\tavailableCounters map[string]counterSets\n\tmutex             sync.RWMutex\n}\n\n// NewAllocator returns an allocator for a certain set of claims or an error if\n// some problem was detected which makes it impossible to allocate claims.\n//\n// The returned Allocator can be used multiple times and is thread-safe.\nfunc NewAllocator(ctx context.Context,\n\tfeatures Features,\n\tallocatedDevices sets.Set[DeviceID],\n\tclassLister DeviceClassLister,\n\tslices []*resourceapi.ResourceSlice,\n\tcelCache *cel.Cache,\n) (*Allocator, error) {\n\treturn &Allocator{\n\t\tfeatures:          features,\n\t\tallocatedDevices:  allocatedDevices,\n\t\tclassLister:       classLister,\n\t\tslices:            slices,\n\t\tcelCache:          celCache,\n\t\tavailableCounters: make(map[string]counterSets),\n\t}, nil\n}\n\nfunc (a *Allocator) Allocate(ctx context.Context, node *v1.Node, claims []*resourceapi.ResourceClaim) (finalResult []resourceapi.AllocationResult, finalErr error) {\n\talloc := &allocator{\n\t\tAllocator:            a,\n\t\tctx:                  ctx, // all methods share the same a and thus ctx\n\t\tlogger:               klog.FromContext(ctx),\n\t\tnode:                 node,\n\t\tclaimsToAllocate:     claims,\n\t\tdeviceMatchesRequest: make(map[matchKey]bool),\n\t\tconstraints:          make([][]constraint, len(claims)),\n\t\tconsumedCounters:     make(map[string]counterSets),\n\t\trequestData:          make(map[requestIndices]requestData),\n\t\tresult:               make([]internalAllocationResult, len(claims)),\n\t}\n\talloc.logger.V(5).Info(\"Starting allocation\", \"numClaims\", len(alloc.claimsToAllocate))\n\tdefer alloc.logger.V(5).Info(\"Done with allocation\", \"success\", len(finalResult) == len(alloc.claimsToAllocate), \"err\", finalErr)\n\n\t// First determine all eligible pools.\n\tpools, err := GatherPools(ctx, alloc.slices, node, a.features)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"gather pool information: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\talloc.pools = pools\n\tif loggerV := alloc.logger.V(7); loggerV.Enabled() {\n\t\tloggerV.Info(\"Gathered pool information\", \"numPools\", len(pools), \"pools\", pools)\n\t} else {\n\t\talloc.logger.V(5).Info(\"Gathered pool information\", \"numPools\", len(pools))\n\t}\n\n\t// We allocate one claim after the other and for each claim, all of\n\t// its requests. For each individual device we pick one possible\n\t// candidate after the other, checking constraints as we go.\n\t// Each chosen candidate is marked as \"in use\" and the process\n\t// continues, recursively. This way, all requests get matched against\n\t// all candidates in all possible orders.\n\t//\n\t// The first full solution is chosen.\n\t//\n\t// In other words, this is an exhaustive search. This is okay because\n\t// it aborts early. Once scoring gets added, more intelligence may be\n\t// needed to avoid trying \"equivalent\" solutions (two identical\n\t// requests, two identical devices, two solutions that are the same in\n\t// practice).\n\n\t// This is where we sanity check that we can actually handle the claims\n\t// and their requests. For each claim we determine how many devices\n\t// need to be allocated. If not all can be stored in the result, the\n\t// claim cannot be allocated.\n\tminDevicesTotal := 0\n\tfor claimIndex, claim := range alloc.claimsToAllocate {\n\t\tminDevicesPerClaim := 0\n\n\t\t// If we have any any request that wants \"all\" devices, we need to\n\t\t// figure out how much \"all\" is. If some pool is incomplete, we stop\n\t\t// here because allocation cannot succeed. Once we do scoring, we should\n\t\t// stop in all cases, not just when \"all\" devices are needed, because\n\t\t// pulling from an incomplete might not pick the best solution and it's\n\t\t// better to wait. This does not matter yet as long the incomplete pool\n\t\t// has some matching device.\n\t\tfor requestIndex := range claim.Spec.Devices.Requests {\n\t\t\trequest := &claim.Spec.Devices.Requests[requestIndex]\n\t\t\trequestKey := requestIndices{claimIndex: claimIndex, requestIndex: requestIndex}\n\t\t\thasSubRequests := len(request.FirstAvailable) > 0\n\n\t\t\t// Error out if the prioritizedList feature is not enabled and the request\n\t\t\t// has subrequests. This is to avoid surprising behavior for users.\n\t\t\tif !a.features.PrioritizedList && hasSubRequests {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: has subrequests, but the DRAPrioritizedList feature is disabled\", klog.KObj(claim), request.Name)\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tif hasSubRequests {\n\t\t\t\t// We need to find the minimum number of devices that can be allocated\n\t\t\t\t// for the request, so setting this to a high number so we can do the\n\t\t\t\t// easy comparison in the loop.\n\t\t\t\tminDevicesPerRequest := math.MaxInt\n\n\t\t\t\t// A request with subrequests gets one entry per subrequest in alloc.requestData.\n\t\t\t\t// We can only predict a lower number of devices because it depends on which\n\t\t\t\t// subrequest gets chosen.\n\t\t\t\tfor i, subReq := range request.FirstAvailable {\n\t\t\t\t\treqData, err := alloc.validateDeviceRequest(&deviceSubRequestAccessor{subRequest: &subReq},\n\t\t\t\t\t\t&exactDeviceRequestAccessor{request: request}, requestKey, pools)\n\t\t\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t\trequestKey.subRequestIndex = i\n\t\t\t\t\talloc.requestData[requestKey] = reqData\n\t\t\t\t\tif reqData.numDevices < minDevicesPerRequest {\n\t\t\t\t\t\tminDevicesPerRequest = reqData.numDevices\n\t\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tminDevicesPerClaim += minDevicesPerRequest\n\t\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t\treqData, err := alloc.validateDeviceRequest(&exactDeviceRequestAccessor{request: request}, nil, requestKey, pools)\n\t\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\talloc.requestData[requestKey] = reqData\n\t\t\t\tminDevicesPerClaim += reqData.numDevices\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\talloc.logger.V(6).Info(\"Checked claim\", \"claim\", klog.KObj(claim), \"minDevices\", minDevicesPerClaim)\n\t\t// Check that we don't end up with too many results.\n\t\t// This isn't perfectly reliable because numDevicesPerClaim is\n\t\t// only a lower bound, so allocation also has to check this.\n\t\tif minDevicesPerClaim > resourceapi.AllocationResultsMaxSize {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s: number of requested devices %d exceeds the claim limit of %d\", klog.KObj(claim), minDevicesPerClaim, resourceapi.AllocationResultsMaxSize)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// If we don't, then we can pre-allocate the result slices for\n\t\t// appending the actual results later.\n\t\talloc.result[claimIndex].devices = make([]internalDeviceResult, 0, minDevicesPerClaim)\n\n\t\t// Constraints are assumed to be monotonic: once a constraint returns\n\t\t// false, adding more devices will not cause it to return true. This\n\t\t// allows the search to stop early once a constraint returns false.\n\t\tconstraints := make([]constraint, len(claim.Spec.Devices.Constraints))\n\t\tfor i, constraint := range claim.Spec.Devices.Constraints {\n\t\t\tswitch {\n\t\t\tcase constraint.MatchAttribute != nil:\n\t\t\t\tmatchAttribute := resourceapi.FullyQualifiedName(*constraint.MatchAttribute)\n\t\t\t\tlogger := alloc.logger\n\t\t\t\tif loggerV := alloc.logger.V(6); loggerV.Enabled() {\n\t\t\t\t\tlogger = klog.LoggerWithName(logger, \"matchAttributeConstraint\")\n\t\t\t\t\tlogger = klog.LoggerWithValues(logger, \"matchAttribute\", matchAttribute)\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tm := &matchAttributeConstraint{\n\t\t\t\t\tlogger:        logger,\n\t\t\t\t\trequestNames:  sets.New(constraint.Requests...),\n\t\t\t\t\tattributeName: matchAttribute,\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tconstraints[i] = m\n\t\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\t\t// Unknown constraint type!\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, constraint #%d: empty constraint (unsupported constraint type?)\", klog.KObj(claim), i)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\talloc.constraints[claimIndex] = constraints\n\t\tminDevicesTotal += minDevicesPerClaim\n\t}\n\n\t// Selecting a device for a request is independent of what has been\n\t// allocated already. Therefore the result of checking a request against\n\t// a device instance in the pool can be cached. The pointer to both\n\t// can serve as key because they are static for the duration of\n\t// the Allocate call and can be compared in Go.\n\talloc.deviceMatchesRequest = make(map[matchKey]bool)\n\n\t// We can estimate the size based on what we need to allocate.\n\talloc.allocatingDevices = make(map[DeviceID]sets.Set[int], minDevicesTotal)\n\n\talloc.logger.V(6).Info(\"Gathered information about devices\", \"numAllocated\", len(alloc.allocatedDevices), \"minDevicesToBeAllocated\", minDevicesTotal)\n\n\t// In practice, there aren't going to be many different CEL\n\t// expressions. Most likely, there is going to be handful of different\n\t// device classes that get used repeatedly. Different requests may all\n\t// use the same selector. Therefore compiling CEL expressions on demand\n\t// could be a useful performance enhancement. It's not implemented yet\n\t// because the key is more complex (just the string?) and the memory\n\t// for both key and cached content is larger than for device matches.\n\t//\n\t// We may also want to cache this in the shared [Allocator] instance,\n\t// which implies adding locking.\n\n\t// All errors get created such that they can be returned by Allocate\n\t// without further wrapping.\n\tdone, err := alloc.allocateOne(deviceIndices{}, false)\n\tif errors.Is(err, errStop) {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tif !done {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\tresult := make([]resourceapi.AllocationResult, len(alloc.result))\n\tfor claimIndex, internalResult := range alloc.result {\n\t\tclaim := alloc.claimsToAllocate[claimIndex]\n\t\tallocationResult := &result[claimIndex]\n\t\tallocationResult.Devices.Results = make([]resourceapi.DeviceRequestAllocationResult, len(internalResult.devices))\n\t\tfor i, internal := range internalResult.devices {\n\t\t\tallocationResult.Devices.Results[i] = resourceapi.DeviceRequestAllocationResult{\n\t\t\t\tRequest:     internal.requestName(),\n\t\t\t\tDriver:      internal.id.Driver.String(),\n\t\t\t\tPool:        internal.id.Pool.String(),\n\t\t\t\tDevice:      internal.id.Device.String(),\n\t\t\t\tAdminAccess: internal.adminAccess,\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Populate configs.\n\t\tfor requestIndex := range claim.Spec.Devices.Requests {\n\t\t\trequestKey := requestIndices{claimIndex: claimIndex, requestIndex: requestIndex}\n\t\t\trequestData := alloc.requestData[requestKey]\n\t\t\tif requestData.parentRequest != nil {\n\t\t\t\t// We need the class of the selected subrequest.\n\t\t\t\trequestKey.subRequestIndex = requestData.selectedSubRequestIndex\n\t\t\t\trequestData = alloc.requestData[requestKey]\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tclass := requestData.class\n\t\t\tif class != nil {\n\t\t\t\tfor _, config := range class.Spec.Config {\n\t\t\t\t\tallocationResult.Devices.Config = append(allocationResult.Devices.Config, resourceapi.DeviceAllocationConfiguration{\n\t\t\t\t\t\tSource:              resourceapi.AllocationConfigSourceClass,\n\t\t\t\t\t\tRequests:            nil, // All of them...\n\t\t\t\t\t\tDeviceConfiguration: config.DeviceConfiguration,\n\t\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor _, config := range claim.Spec.Devices.Config {\n\t\t\t// If Requests are empty, it applies to all. So it can just be included.\n\t\t\tif len(config.Requests) == 0 {\n\t\t\t\tallocationResult.Devices.Config = append(allocationResult.Devices.Config, resourceapi.DeviceAllocationConfiguration{\n\t\t\t\t\tSource:              resourceapi.AllocationConfigSourceClaim,\n\t\t\t\t\tRequests:            config.Requests,\n\t\t\t\t\tDeviceConfiguration: config.DeviceConfiguration,\n\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tfor i, request := range claim.Spec.Devices.Requests {\n\t\t\t\tif slices.Contains(config.Requests, request.Name) {\n\t\t\t\t\tallocationResult.Devices.Config = append(allocationResult.Devices.Config, resourceapi.DeviceAllocationConfiguration{\n\t\t\t\t\t\tSource:              resourceapi.AllocationConfigSourceClaim,\n\t\t\t\t\t\tRequests:            config.Requests,\n\t\t\t\t\t\tDeviceConfiguration: config.DeviceConfiguration,\n\t\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\t\trequestKey := requestIndices{claimIndex: claimIndex, requestIndex: i}\n\t\t\t\trequestData := alloc.requestData[requestKey]\n\t\t\t\tif requestData.parentRequest == nil {\n\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\t\tsubRequest := request.FirstAvailable[requestData.selectedSubRequestIndex]\n\t\t\t\tsubRequestName := fmt.Sprintf(\"%s/%s\", request.Name, subRequest.Name)\n\t\t\t\tif slices.Contains(config.Requests, subRequestName) {\n\t\t\t\t\tallocationResult.Devices.Config = append(allocationResult.Devices.Config, resourceapi.DeviceAllocationConfiguration{\n\t\t\t\t\t\tSource:              resourceapi.AllocationConfigSourceClaim,\n\t\t\t\t\t\tRequests:            config.Requests,\n\t\t\t\t\t\tDeviceConfiguration: config.DeviceConfiguration,\n\t\t\t\t\t})\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Determine node selector.\n\t\tnodeSelector, err := alloc.createNodeSelector(internalResult.devices)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"create NodeSelector for claim %s: %w\", claim.Name, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tallocationResult.NodeSelector = nodeSelector\n\t}\n\n\treturn result, nil\n}\n\nfunc (a *Allocator) GetStats() *Stats {\n\t// Not implemented.\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (alloc *allocator) validateDeviceRequest(request requestAccessor, parentRequest requestAccessor, requestKey requestIndices, pools []*Pool) (requestData, error) {\n\tclaim := alloc.claimsToAllocate[requestKey.claimIndex]\n\trequestData := requestData{\n\t\trequest:       request,\n\t\tparentRequest: parentRequest,\n\t}\n\tfor i, selector := range request.selectors() {\n\t\tif selector.CEL == nil {\n\t\t\t// Unknown future selector type!\n\t\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s, selector #%d: CEL expression empty (unsupported selector type?)\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), i)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif !alloc.features.AdminAccess && request.hasAdminAccess() {\n\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: admin access is requested, but the feature is disabled\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name())\n\t}\n\n\t// Should be set. If it isn't, something changed and we should refuse to proceed.\n\tif request.deviceClassName() == \"\" {\n\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: missing device class name (unsupported request type?)\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name())\n\t}\n\tclass, err := alloc.classLister.Get(request.deviceClassName())\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: could not retrieve device class %s: %w\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), request.deviceClassName(), err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Start collecting information about the request.\n\t// The class must be set and stored before calling isSelectable.\n\trequestData.class = class\n\n\tswitch request.allocationMode() {\n\tcase resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeExactCount:\n\t\tnumDevices := request.count()\n\t\tif numDevices > math.MaxInt {\n\t\t\t// Allowed by API validation, but doesn't make sense.\n\t\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: exact count %d is too large\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), numDevices)\n\t\t}\n\t\trequestData.numDevices = int(numDevices)\n\tcase resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeAll:\n\t\t// If we have any any request that wants \"all\" devices, we need to\n\t\t// figure out how much \"all\" is. If some pool is incomplete, we stop\n\t\t// here because allocation cannot succeed. Once we do scoring, we should\n\t\t// stop in all cases, not just when \"all\" devices are needed, because\n\t\t// pulling from an incomplete might not pick the best solution and it's\n\t\t// better to wait. This does not matter yet as long the incomplete pool\n\t\t// has some matching device.\n\t\trequestData.allDevices = make([]deviceWithID, 0, resourceapi.AllocationResultsMaxSize)\n\t\tfor _, pool := range pools {\n\t\t\tif pool.IsIncomplete {\n\t\t\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently being updated\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), pool.PoolID)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif pool.IsInvalid {\n\t\t\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently invalid\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), pool.PoolID)\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tfor _, slice := range pool.Slices {\n\t\t\t\tfor deviceIndex := range slice.Spec.Devices {\n\t\t\t\t\tselectable, err := alloc.isSelectable(requestKey, requestData, slice, deviceIndex)\n\t\t\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\t\t\treturn requestData, err\n\t\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t\tif selectable {\n\t\t\t\t\t\tdevice := deviceWithID{\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\tid:     DeviceID{Driver: slice.Spec.Driver, Pool: slice.Spec.Pool.Name, Device: slice.Spec.Devices[deviceIndex].Name},\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\tDevice: &slice.Spec.Devices[deviceIndex],\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\tslice:  slice,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t\t\trequestData.allDevices = append(requestData.allDevices, device)\n\t\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\trequestData.numDevices = len(requestData.allDevices)\n\t\talloc.logger.V(6).Info(\"Request for 'all' devices\", \"claim\", klog.KObj(claim), \"request\", request.name(), \"numDevicesPerRequest\", requestData.numDevices)\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: unsupported count mode %s\", klog.KObj(claim), request.name(), request.allocationMode())\n\t}\n\treturn requestData, nil\n}\n\n// errStop is a special error that gets returned by allocateOne if it detects\n// that allocation cannot succeed.\nvar errStop = errors.New(\"stop allocation\")\n\n// allocator is used while an [Allocator.Allocate] is running. Only a single\n// goroutine works with it, so there is no need for locking.\ntype allocator struct {\n\t*Allocator\n\tctx                  context.Context\n\tlogger               klog.Logger\n\tnode                 *v1.Node\n\tclaimsToAllocate     []*resourceapi.ResourceClaim\n\tpools                []*Pool\n\tdeviceMatchesRequest map[matchKey]bool\n\tconstraints          [][]constraint // one list of constraints per claim\n\t// consumedCounters keeps track of the counters consumed by all devices\n\t// that are in the process of being allocated.\n\t// The keys in the map are ResourceSlice names.\n\tconsumedCounters map[string]counterSets\n\trequestData      map[requestIndices]requestData // one entry per request with no subrequests and one entry per subrequest\n\t// allocatingDevices tracks which devices will be newly allocated for a\n\t// particular attempt to find a solution. The map is indexed by device\n\t// and its values represent for which of a pod's claims the device will\n\t// be allocated.\n\t// Claims are identified by their index in claimsToAllocate.\n\tallocatingDevices map[DeviceID]sets.Set[int]\n\tresult            []internalAllocationResult\n}\n\n// counterSets is a map with the name of counter sets to the counters in\n// the set.\ntype counterSets map[draapi.UniqueString]map[string]resourceapi.Counter\n\n// matchKey identifies a device/request pair.\ntype matchKey struct {\n\tDeviceID\n\trequestIndices\n}\n\n// requestIndices identifies one specific request\n// or subrequest by three properties:\n//\n// - claimIndex: The index of the claim in the requestData map.\n// - requestIndex: The index of the request in the claim.\n// - subRequestIndex: The index of the subrequest in the parent request.\ntype requestIndices struct {\n\tclaimIndex, requestIndex int\n\tsubRequestIndex          int\n}\n\n// deviceIndices identifies one specific required device inside\n// a request or subrequest of a certain claim.\ntype deviceIndices struct {\n\tclaimIndex      int // The index of the claim in the allocator.\n\trequestIndex    int // The index of the request in the claim.\n\tsubRequestIndex int // The index of the subrequest within the request (ignored if subRequest is false).\n\tdeviceIndex     int // The index of a device within a request or subrequest.","numLines":500,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1407}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01Qys7CG9GrAMeyxfW73vdES","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01KPsYDefRKtzHErSxU1yho7","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/investigation.md","content":"# Investigation Report: DRA AllocationMode.All Multi-Node Resource Pool Support\n\n## Summary\n\nThe Dynamic Resource Allocation (DRA) scheduler plugin is being modified (commit 2e534d6da10) to allow `AllocationMode: All` requests from multi-node resource pools, which were previously restricted to single-node pools only. This change modifies pool completeness detection to examine global pool state across all ResourceSlice objects before node filtering, enabling allocation of \"all\" devices from pools spanning multiple nodes. The change affects scheduler hot paths, allocator logic, and kubelet device management, with potential performance implications for the device selection algorithm.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n**The Change:** The DRA allocator previously marked multi-node pools as \"incomplete\" during allocation when `AllocationMode: All` was requested. This restriction was enforced by checking pool completeness **after** filtering slices for the target node.\n\n**Why It Was Restricted:** When only node-specific slices are examined, the allocator cannot verify if all pool slices have been received (ResourceSliceCount field cannot be validated). This created uncertainty about whether \"all\" devices were truly being allocated.\n\n**The Fix:** The change moves pool completeness validation to occur **before** node filtering. The `GatherPools()` function now:\n1. Groups all ResourceSlice objects by pool identity (driver + pool name) globally\n2. Checks ResourceSliceCount consistency across ALL slices for each pool\n3. Verifies generation field alignment across all slices\n4. Marks pools as incomplete/valid before any node-based filtering\n5. Allows `AllocationMode: All` from complete multi-node pools\n\n**Why This Matters:** This enables users to request \"all\" devices from a pool that spans multiple nodes (e.g., a network-attached resource shared across the cluster), rather than being limited to node-local device pools.\n\n## Evidence\n\n### Source Files Modified\n\n**File:** `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable/pools_stable.go` (Commit 2e534d6da10, lines 48-94)\n\n**Key Changes:**\n- **Line 48-50:** New `poolIdentifier` type introduced to track pools globally by (driver, pool name)\n- **Line 59-66:** First pass creates `slicesByPool` map grouping all ResourceSlices by pool identity across the entire cluster\n- **Line 68-95:** Pool completeness checking performed globally:\n  - Line 72-89: For each pool, verifies `ResourceSliceCount` matches actual slice count (line 80) and generation consistency (line 86)\n  - Line 92-94: Mark pools as incomplete if verification fails\n- **Line 143:** Pool.IsIncomplete now set based on global completeness check instead of local node filtering\n\n### Allocator Logic Using AllocationMode.All\n\n**File:** `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable/allocator_stable.go`\n\n**Lines 402-436:** The `validateDeviceRequest()` function handles `DeviceAllocationModeAll`:\n```\ncase resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeAll:\n    // Lines 411-414: Rejects incomplete pools for AllocationMode: All\n    if pool.IsIncomplete {\n        return error: \"asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently being updated\"\n    }\n    // Lines 419-434: Iterates through ALL pool slices and collects matching devices\n    for _, slice := range pool.Slices {\n        for deviceIndex := range slice.Spec.Devices {\n            selectable, err := alloc.isSelectable(...)\n            if selectable {\n                requestData.allDevices = append(...)\n            }\n        }\n    }\n```\n\n**Critical Insight:** The allocator collects devices from **all slices in the pool** (line 419), not just the target node's slices. This is the key difference from `ExactCount` mode.\n\n### Test Coverage Added\n\n**File:** `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/allocatortesting/allocator_testing.go` (Commit 2e534d6da10)\n\n**Test Case:** `\"allocation-mode-all-with-multi-host-resource-pool\"`\n- Two ResourceSlice objects for pool1 on different nodes (node1, node2)\n- ResourceSliceCount=2 (indicating 2 slices expected)\n- Generation field consistent across slices\n- Request with `AllocationMode: All` should successfully allocate devices from both slices\n\n### API Validation\n\n**File:** `pkg/apis/resource/validation/validation.go` (lines 268-286)\n\nThe API validation enforces count field rules but **does not** prevent `AllocationMode: All` on multi-node pools:\n```go\ncase resource.DeviceAllocationModeAll:\n    if count != 0 {\n        // Error: count must not be specified\n    }\n```\n\nThe multi-node restriction is enforced **at allocator level**, not API level. This means:\n- Invalid ResourceClaims with `AllocationMode: All` on incomplete pools can be created\n- The scheduler will reject allocation with error \"currently being updated\"\n- The pod becomes unschedulable until the pool becomes complete\n\n## Affected Components\n\n### High Impact (Scheduler Hot Path)\n\n1. **DRA Scheduler Plugin - Pool Gathering**\n   - **File:** `pkg/scheduler/framework/plugins/dynamicresources/dynamicresources.go:627-679`\n   - **Component:** `PreFilter()` phase calls `GatherAllocatedState()` and creates new allocator\n   - **Impact:** For every pod-node pair, `GatherPools()` is called. With the change, all ResourceSlices are now examined before filtering, potentially increasing memory and CPU usage\n   - **Risk Level:** MEDIUM - This is in the hot path of scheduling every pod with DRA claims\n   - **Performance Concern:** Grouping all slices globally (line 59-66 of pools_stable.go) before filtering adds overhead proportional to total number of slices in the cluster\n\n2. **Allocator - Device Selection (validateDeviceRequest)**\n   - **File:** `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable/allocator_stable.go:364-442`\n   - **Component:** Called during Filter phase for each claim's device request\n   - **Impact:** Lines 411-436 iterate through all pools and all their slices for `AllocationMode: All`\n   - **Risk Level:** MEDIUM - Iteration across multiple slices increases allocation time\n   - **Complexity:** O(pools × slices_per_pool × devices_per_slice) vs previous O(node_slices × devices)\n\n3. **Allocated Devices Cache**\n   - **File:** `pkg/scheduler/framework/plugins/dynamicresources/allocateddevices.go:87-156`\n   - **Component:** Thread-safe cache of allocated devices accessed in Filter phase\n   - **Impact:** Multi-node pools mean devices are tracked across more slices, cache size may increase\n   - **Risk Level:** LOW - Cache is already designed for global device tracking\n\n### Medium Impact (Scheduler Decision Making)\n\n4. **DRA Scheduler Filter Phase**\n   - **File:** `pkg/scheduler/framework/plugins/dynamicresources/dynamicresources.go:884-1019`\n   - **Component:** Called per pod-node combination, attempts allocation\n   - **Impact:** Now may attempt larger device allocations (all devices from multi-node pool)\n   - **Risk Level:** MEDIUM - More devices to allocate per claim = longer Filter phase\n   - **Potential Issue:** Larger allocation results could exceed `AllocationResultsMaxSize` (line 201-202)\n\n5. **ResourceClaim Status Updates**\n   - **File:** `pkg/scheduler/framework/plugins/dynamicresources/dynamicresources.go:1290-1348`\n   - **Component:** PreBind phase updates claim allocation status\n   - **Impact:** Allocation results may contain more devices\n   - **Risk Level:** LOW - PreBind executes after scheduling decision, not on hot path\n\n### Lower Impact (API and Validation)\n\n6. **API Server Validation**\n   - **File:** `pkg/apis/resource/validation/validation.go`\n   - **Component:** Validates `DeviceAllocationMode` and count field\n   - **Impact:** No API changes needed; allocator-level enforcement is sufficient\n   - **Risk Level:** VERY LOW - No changes to API contract\n\n7. **Kubelet Allocation Management**\n   - **File:** `pkg/kubelet/allocation/allocation_manager.go`\n   - **Component:** Stores allocation state, maps pods to devices\n   - **Impact:** May store more devices per claim, but same checkpoint-based mechanism\n   - **Risk Level:** LOW - Kubelet just reads what scheduler writes\n\n### Lower Impact (Device Plugins)\n\n8. **Device Plugins and ResourceSlice Controllers**\n   - **File:** Device plugins are external components\n   - **Component:** Provide ResourceSlice objects and device information\n   - **Impact:** No changes to plugin behavior, but plugins that create multi-node pools will now support AllocationMode: All\n   - **Risk Level:** LOW - Plugins are unchanged\n\n## Performance Implications\n\n### Scheduler Hot Path Analysis\n\n**Before the change:**\n- Pool completeness check: O(1) - only examine node-specific slices\n- Pool.IsIncomplete = `len(pool.Slices) != pool.Slices[0].Spec.Pool.ResourceSliceCount`\n- For multi-node pools, always marked incomplete for AllocationMode: All\n\n**After the change:**\n- Pool completeness check: O(N) - examine all slices in cluster to group by pool\n  - N = total number of ResourceSlice objects globally\n  - Must iterate all slices and build `slicesByPool` map (lines 59-66)\n  - Must iterate each pool's slices to verify consistency (lines 72-89)\n- Example: 100 nodes × 10 slices/node = 1000 slices, examined for every pod allocation\n\n### Critical Hot Path: GatherPools() Execution Time\n\n**Location:** `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable/pools_stable.go:58-149`\n\n**Called from:**\n- `Allocator.Allocate()` line 114 - **every pod scheduling attempt**\n- Executed once per Pod × Node pair in Filter phase\n- For a 100-node cluster with 1000 pods: **100,000 calls** during a single scheduling cycle\n\n**New Operations (lines 59-95):**\n1. Build `slicesByPool` map: O(total slices) - **NEW OVERHEAD**\n2. Iterate pools to verify completeness: O(total slices) - **NEW OVERHEAD**\n3. Build `incompletePools` set lookup: O(total pools) - **NEW OVERHEAD**\n\n**Impact Calculation:**\n- Assuming 1000 slices, 50 pools: adds ~1000 iterations per scheduling cycle\n- × 100,000 scheduling attempts per full cluster scheduling = **100 million iterations**\n- At 1 microsecond per iteration: **100 seconds overhead per scheduling cycle**\n\n### Memory Impact\n\n- **slicesByPool** map: O(total slices) size - potentially gigabytes for large clusters\n- **incompletePools** set: O(pools) size - minimal impact\n\n## Downstream Consumer Impact\n\n### Kubelet Device Allocation\n\n**File:** `pkg/kubelet/allocation/allocation_manager.go`\n\n**Impact Level:** LOW\n- Kubelet reads `ResourceClaim.Status.Allocation.Devices.Results[]` written by scheduler\n- No changes to kubelet logic required\n- May store more device entries per claim, but checkpoint mechanism is designed for this\n- Kubelet state files could grow if multi-node allocations are common\n\n### Pod Admission and Extended Resources\n\n**File:** `pkg/scheduler/framework/plugins/dynamicresources/dynamicresources.go:863`\n\n**Impact Level:** MEDIUM\n- For extended resources created from DRA claims, scheduler creates `ExactDeviceRequest` with count=N\n- Multi-node pools mean more devices allocated, more extended resources created\n- Pod.Spec.Containers[].Resources.Requests.extended-resources could grow larger\n- Kubelet must configure all allocated devices (GPU, networking, etc.)\n\n### Device Plugin Configuration\n\n**Impact Level:** LOW to MEDIUM\n- Device plugins execute in response to kubelet requesting device setup\n- More devices per pod = more device plugin operations per pod\n- Device plugin communication overhead increases linearly with device count\n\n## Risk Assessment\n\n### Critical Risks\n\n**1. Performance Regression in Scheduler (HIGH PROBABILITY, SEVERE IMPACT)**\n\n**Evidence:** The change moves expensive pool gathering to a hot path executed 100,000+ times per scheduling cycle. Building the `slicesByPool` map is O(total slices in cluster), not O(slices for target node).\n\n**Example:**\n- 200 nodes, 5 slices per node = 1000 total slices\n- 100 pods to schedule on 100 nodes = 10,000 filter attempts minimum\n- Each filter creates new allocator, calls GatherPools, iterates 1000 slices\n- = 10 million slice iterations, potentially 10+ seconds added latency per scheduling cycle\n\n**Mitigation Needed:**\n- Cache pool completeness state at cluster level, not per-node\n- Only recompute when ResourceSlice objects change\n- Use informer event-driven updates instead of re-gathering in hot path\n\n**Test Plan Required:**\n- Benchmark scheduler performance with 100+ nodes and multi-node resource pools\n- Measure scheduling latency for workloads using AllocationMode: All\n- Profile CPU and memory during full cluster scheduling\n\n---\n\n**2. Incomplete Pool Allocation Failure (MEDIUM PROBABILITY, HIGH IMPACT)**\n\n**Scenario:** Resource controllers update multi-node pools by creating new ResourceSlice objects. During the update window:\n- New slice is created but not yet seen by scheduler\n- Pool's ResourceSliceCount != actual slice count\n- Any pod with `AllocationMode: All` claim becomes unschedulable with error \"currently being updated\"\n- Pod may remain unschedulable for duration of controller update\n\n**Evidence:** Allocator logic (line 412-413) rejects incomplete pools:\n```go\nif pool.IsIncomplete {\n    return requestData, fmt.Errorf(\"claim %s, request %s: asks for all devices,\n        but resource pool %s is currently being updated\", ...)\n}\n```\n\n**Mitigation Needed:**\n- Document pool update procedure and timing windows\n- Implement exponential backoff in scheduler for unschedulable pods waiting on pool updates\n- Add metrics to track allocation failures due to incomplete pools\n\n**Test Plan Required:**\n- Simulate ResourceSlice controller updates during scheduling\n- Verify pod recovers when pool becomes complete\n- Measure time pod spends unschedulable\n\n---\n\n**3. API/Allocator Mismatch (LOW PROBABILITY, MEDIUM IMPACT)**\n\n**Issue:** API validation does not prevent `AllocationMode: All` on multi-node pools. If allocator code is rolled back but API accepts these claims, scheduler will fail with confusing error.\n\n**Evidence:**\n- Validation (pkg/apis/resource/validation/validation.go:268-286) only checks count field\n- No validation rule prevents multi-node pools for AllocationMode: All\n- Allocator enforcement only happens during scheduling, not at claim creation\n\n**Mitigation Needed:**\n- Add API-level validation webhook to reject problematic claims\n- Or clearly document this is allocator-enforced, not API-enforced\n- Or add feature gate to API validation\n\n**Test Plan Required:**\n- Verify ResourceClaim creation rejects AllocationMode: All on incomplete/multi-node pools\n- Test error messages guide users to fix claims\n\n---\n\n### Medium Risks\n\n**4. DeviceAllocationResult Size Explosion (MEDIUM PROBABILITY, MEDIUM IMPACT)**\n\n**Scenario:** `AllocationMode: All` from multi-node pool allocates 100+ devices. ResourceClaim.Status.Allocation.Devices.Results grows to maximum:\n\n```go\n// Line 201-202 of allocator_stable.go\nif minDevicesPerClaim > resourceapi.AllocationResultsMaxSize {\n    return nil, fmt.Errorf(\"claim: number of requested devices %d exceeds limit %d\", ...)\n}\n```\n\nCurrent limit is likely 127 or 255 devices per claim. Multi-node pools could exceed this.\n\n**Evidence:** Commit 2e534d6da10 test case shows pool spanning 2 nodes with 2+ devices each. No limit is enforced on multi-node pool device count.\n\n**Mitigation Needed:**\n- Document AllocationMode: All maximum device count in resource pool design guidance\n- Add scheduler metric tracking allocation result sizes\n- Consider dynamic limit based on available memory\n\n**Test Plan Required:**\n- Test claim allocation with 50, 100, 200 devices\n- Verify error messages when limit exceeded\n- Monitor ResourceClaim object sizes in etcd\n\n---\n\n**5. Concurrent Update Safety (LOW PROBABILITY, MEDIUM IMPACT)**\n\n**Scenario:** Multiple schedulers (for high availability) examine pools while resource controller is updating. Race condition between pool completeness check and actual device allocation.\n\n**Example:**\n- Scheduler A gathers pools, sees ResourceSliceCount=2, len(slices)=2, marks complete\n- Resource controller updates pool, removes device from slice 1\n- Scheduler A allocates device that no longer exists\n- Device allocation fails when pod is bound\n\n**Mitigation Needed:**\n- Document scheduler behavior under concurrent updates\n- Add transaction-like behavior to ResourceClaim allocation\n- Use generation field to detect mid-flight updates\n\n**Test Plan Required:**\n- Simulate multiple scheduler instances with ResourceSlice controller updates\n- Verify allocation consistency\n- Test error recovery and pod rescheduling\n\n---\n\n### Lower Risks\n\n**6. Kubelet Device Configuration Overhead (LOW PROBABILITY, LOW IMPACT)**\n\nDevice plugins and kubelet must process more devices per pod. Not a correctness issue, but operational complexity increases.\n\n**7. Extended Resource API Changes (LOW PROBABILITY, LOW IMPACT)**\n\nExtended resources created from DRA have larger limits if allocating \"all\" devices. Kubernetes API objects grow, but no breaking changes.\n\n---\n\n## Testing Requirements Before Release\n\n### Unit Test Coverage\n- ✅ Existing: Test case \"allocation-mode-all-with-multi-host-resource-pool\" added (commit 2e534d6da10)\n- ❌ TODO: Test pool completeness during ResourceSlice updates\n- ❌ TODO: Test scheduler behavior with incomplete pools\n- ❌ TODO: Test allocation failure scenarios\n\n### Integration Tests\n- ❌ TODO: Multi-node resource pool creation and allocation\n- ❌ TODO: Pod lifecycle with multi-node allocated devices\n- ❌ TODO: Kubelet device configuration for multi-node allocations\n- ❌ TODO: Device plugin communication for multi-node devices\n\n### Performance Benchmarks\n- ❌ TODO: Scheduler Filter phase latency with 100+ nodes and multi-node pools\n- ❌ TODO: Allocator performance with varying pool sizes and device counts\n- ❌ TODO: Memory usage growth with large allocation results\n\n### Stress/Edge Case Tests\n- ❌ TODO: Rapid ResourceSlice updates during pod scheduling\n- ❌ TODO: Scheduler behavior under high pod churn\n- ❌ TODO: Allocation with maximum device count (127/255 limit)\n- ❌ TODO: Multiple schedulers with concurrent allocation attempts\n\n### Failure Recovery Tests\n- ❌ TODO: Pod recovery when pool becomes complete after update\n- ❌ TODO: Device plugin failure handling for multi-node allocations\n- ❌ TODO: Kubelet restart with multi-node allocated devices\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Risk Mitigation Strategy\n\n1. **PRIORITY 1: Address Performance Regression**\n   - Profile `GatherPools()` with realistic cluster sizes (100+ nodes, 5+ slices/node)\n   - If latency > 10ms per invocation, implement caching:\n     - Keep pool completeness state in scheduler plugin\n     - Invalidate on ResourceSlice watch events\n     - Avoid re-gathering for every pod\n   - Benchmark before/after to quantify improvement\n\n2. **PRIORITY 2: Implement Operator Guidance**\n   - Document when/how to use `AllocationMode: All` with multi-node pools\n   - Create runbook for handling \"pool currently being updated\" errors\n   - Add observability: metrics for incomplete pool allocation attempts\n\n3. **PRIORITY 3: Complete Test Coverage**\n   - Add e2e tests for multi-node pool allocation lifecycle\n   - Implement chaos tests: simulate ResourceSlice updates during scheduling\n   - Add performance regression tests to CI/CD pipeline\n\n4. **PRIORITY 4: Consider API-Level Safeguards**\n   - Add validation webhook to prevent problematic claims\n   - Or add feature gate (e.g., DRAMultiNodeAllocationMode) to gate the change\n   - Document that this is allocator-enforced, not API-enforced\n\n### Phased Rollout Plan\n\n**Phase 1: Alpha (This Release)**\n- ✅ Merge commit 2e534d6da10 with feature gate disabled by default\n- Feature gate: `DRAMultiNodeAllocationMode=false` in alpha release\n- Allows early adopters to test in controlled environments\n\n**Phase 2: Beta (Next Release)**\n- Evaluate performance test results\n- Enable feature gate by default if no regressions found\n- Extend test coverage based on Phase 1 learnings\n\n**Phase 3: GA (Future Release)**\n- Remove feature gate\n- Ensure all downstream systems (kubelet, device plugins) fully support multi-node allocations\n\n### Approval Checklist Before Merge\n\n- [ ] Performance benchmarks completed; GatherPools() latency < 10ms per invocation\n- [ ] Multi-node pool allocation end-to-end test passes\n- [ ] Feature gate implemented and tested in both states\n- [ ] Documentation updated with usage guidelines\n- [ ] Failure scenarios tested: incomplete pools, device limit, concurrent updates\n- [ ] Kubelet and device plugin integration tested\n- [ ] Scheduler stress tests pass with multi-node workloads\n\n## Conclusion\n\nThe change to allow `AllocationMode: All` from multi-node resource pools is **functionally correct** and addresses a real user need (allocation of non-local resources). This change modifies pool completeness detection to examine global pool state across all ResourceSlice objects before node filtering, enabling allocation of \"all\" devices from pools spanning multiple nodes. The change affects scheduler hot paths, allocator logic, and kubelet device management, with potential performance implications for the device selection algorithm.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n**The Change:** The DRA allocator previously marked multi-node pools as \"incomplete\" during allocation when `AllocationMode: All` was requested. This restriction was enforced by checking pool completeness **after** filtering slices for the target node.\n\n**Why It Was Restricted:** When only node-specific slices are examined, the allocator cannot verify if all pool slices have been received (ResourceSliceCount field cannot be validated). This created uncertainty about whether \"all\" devices were truly being allocated.\n\n**The Fix:** The change moves pool completeness validation to occur **before** node filtering. The `GatherPools()` function now:\n1. Groups all ResourceSlice objects by pool identity (driver + pool name) globally\n2. Checks ResourceSliceCount consistency across ALL slices for each pool\n3. Verifies generation field alignment across all slices\n4. Marks pools as incomplete/valid before any node-based filtering\n5. Allows `AllocationMode: All` from complete multi-node pools\n\n**Why This Matters:** This enables users to request \"all\" devices from a pool that spans multiple nodes (e.g., a network-attached resource shared across the cluster), rather than being limited to node-local device pools.\n\n## Evidence\n\n### Source Files Modified\n\n**File:** `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable/pools_stable.go` (Commit 2e534d6da10, lines 48-94)\n\n**Key Changes:**\n- **Line 48-50:** New `poolIdentifier` type introduced to track pools globally by (driver, pool name)\n- **Line 59-66:** First pass creates `slicesByPool` map grouping all ResourceSlices by pool identity across the entire cluster\n- **Line 68-95:** Pool completeness checking performed globally:\n  - Line 72-89: For each pool, verifies `ResourceSliceCount` matches actual slice count (line 80) and generation consistency (line 86)\n  - Line 92-94: Mark pools as incomplete if verification fails\n- **Line 143:** Pool.IsIncomplete now set based on global completeness check instead of local node filtering\n\n### Allocator Logic Using AllocationMode.All\n\n**File:** `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/stable/allocator_stable.go`\n\n**Lines 402-436:** The `validateDeviceRequest()` function handles `DeviceAllocationModeAll`:\n```\ncase resourceapi.DeviceAllocationModeAll:\n    // Lines 411-414: Rejects incomplete pools for AllocationMode: All\n    if pool.IsIncomplete {\n        return error: \"asks for all devices, but resource pool %s is currently being updated\"\n    }\n    // Lines 419-434: Iterates through ALL pool slices and collects matching devices\n    for _, slice := range pool.Slices {\n        for deviceIndex := range slice.Spec.Devices {\n            selectable, err := alloc.isSelectable(...)\n            if selectable {\n                requestData.allDevices = append(...)\n            }\n        }\n    }\n```\n\n**Critical Insight:** The allocator collects devices from **all slices in the pool** (line 419), not just the target node's slices. This is the key difference from `ExactCount` mode.\n\n### Test Coverage Added\n\n**File:** `staging/src/k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation/structured/internal/allocatortesting/allocator_testing.go` (Commit 2e534d6da10)\n\n**Test Case:** `\"allocation-mode-all-with-multi-host-resource-pool\"`\n- Two ResourceSlice objects for pool1 on different nodes (node1, node2)\n- ResourceSliceCount=2 (indicating 2 slices expected)\n- Generation field consistent across slices\n- Request with `AllocationMode: All` should successfully allocate devices from both slices\n\n### API Validation\n\n**File:** `pkg/apis/resource/validation/validation.go` (lines 268-286)\n\nThe API validation enforces count field rules but **does not** prevent `AllocationMode: All` on multi-node pools:\n```go\ncase resource.DeviceAllocationModeAll:\n    if count != 0 {\n        // Error: count must not be specified\n    }\n```\n\nThe multi-node restriction is enforced **at allocator level**, not API level.
